Frozen verdicts (rules unchanged): all as before EXCEPT `branch_coil` x `beta1_unmasked`, REJECTED -> WORTH PURSUING (resolved +26 points at accuracy 0.937); that is 1 of 3 diagnosable slots, so all three levers remain
"not worth pursuing overall". **Corrections to Addendum 12's reading:** the plain rule is NOT overconfident to the degree reported -- `ahu_vav` is exactly right when it answers (0 wrong confident verdicts) but resolves 59%
(not 100% in ~2 reads); `coil_ahu`'s accuracy among resolved is 0.79 on only 15 answers (not 0.67); `branch_coil` is 0.85. What Addendum 12 got right and the fix leaves standing: calibrated tempering on `loop_branch` is
what keeps every confident verdict right (removing it: 35% wrong-and-confident); the budget is not the limit; masking off is not free (`coil_ahu` 0.66).

**Result of Part B (2026-09-24, `scripts/47`, 165 s, run once; the consistency check needed Deviation 1 above): the reliability layer is nearly silent, so it "silences" rather than fixes -- but the overconfidence it
was meant to cure was mostly not there.** Plain rule (`none`, the frozen pipeline's episodes) per target slot: accuracy among answered `branch_coil` 0.909 [cluster CI 0.65, 1.00] (32/105 answered), `coil_ahu` 0.833 [0.50, 1.00] (16/44), `ahu_vav` 1.000
(34/56); control `loop_branch` 1.000 (14/40). Layer `new`: answered `branch_coil` 0/105, `coil_ahu` 2/44 (accuracy 0.50, one of the two wrong), `ahu_vav` 0/56, `loop_branch` 2/40 (1.00); pooled over all 11 slots 8/585 answered (accuracy 0.917
[0.62, 1.00], wrong-and-confident 0.2%), retaining 3% of the plain rule's correct verdicts. Layer `legacy` (descriptive): 39/585 answered, accuracy 1.000, retained 17%. **Frozen verdicts:** `branch_coil` NO PROBLEM TO FIX (plain accuracy
0.909 >= 0.90), `coil_ahu` ABSTAINS -- too few answers to judge (honest but empty), `ahu_vav` NO PROBLEM TO FIX; overall "the overconfidence was mostly an artefact of the defect"; control: no regression.
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (1) `branch_coil` clears the 0.90 line by 0.009 and its interval spans 0.65-1.00; Part A's `base` on a DIFFERENT (equally valid) draw of matched-correct negatives gave 0.849 for the same slot
(`coil_ahu` 0.792 vs 0.833; `ahu_vav` and `loop_branch` 1.000 both) -- that spread IS the sampling noise in the right-document class, and it is as large as the gap being judged, so the `branch_coil` and `coil_ahu` calls
are not resolved by this data. (2) What is solid: `ahu_vav` and `loop_branch` answers were all correct in both harnesses, and the layer is unusable as frozen on this data: it refused EVERY plain-rule answer in 3 of the 8 folds (held out
`sys03_psz_ac`, `dev_F`, `dev_G`: "no honest confidence level demonstrated by out-of-unit runs", 93 answers) and answered only 8 of the remaining ~175 (the abstention labels, e.g. "near-twin", are heuristic explanations picked
after the score falls below the cut, not diagnoses). It discards genuinely correct verdicts (`ahu_vav`: 34 correct plain answers -> 0). (3) *Exploratory, NOT pre-registered, NOT part of any verdict:* pooled over all slots the plain rule's accuracy
among answered is 0.869 [0.82, 0.92] against a stated 0.95, and the shortfall sits in slots the addendum did not target -- `coil_vav` 0.72 (58 of 80 answered) and `vav_dat` 0.85 (95 of 96) -- where the layer answered 0/80 and 4/96;
`vav_zone` answers 0/128 under both rules (Addendums 11 and 13). Any follow-up on `coil_vav`/`vav_dat` needs its own pre-registration.
**Consequences flagged, not acted on:** (a) `scripts/37` and `scripts/38` (and unaudited `scripts/13`) carry the same omission and their conclusions (Addenda 6, 7) are un-audited; (b) `diagnose_connection`
does not use the reliability layer (and still applies `apply_trust_weights`, Addendum 8's inconsistency), so this result does not change what it returns; (c) the Sep-21 frozen manifest (`results/docmismatch/active/frozen/manifest.json`) no longer matches
four files of `active/` (`calibrate.py`, `engine.py`, `model.py`, `pools.py`): `calibrate.py` and `engine.py` already differ in the first commit, i.e. they were edited after the freeze (cause not investigated here), and `model.py` / `pools.py`
also changed with Addendum 9's opt-in closure channel (default behaviour tested bit-identical). `readiness.frozen_check` would therefore refuse a new sealed run until re-frozen; the `sealed_run_*.done` markers are dated Sep 21, and nothing here touches SEALED data.

## Addendum 15 (dated 2026-09-24, written BEFORE generating the new campaigns or computing any score on them): how robust is the dither campaign, and does wiring it into the engine make `vav_zone` verdicts better?

**Motivation (discussed with the user; the user asked for this addendum after we agreed `vav_zone` is the largest unresolved slot).** Addendum 13 showed, in simulation, that dithering each zone's setpoint identifies its serving box where passive data cannot
(0.97 at 96 h July; 0.89 at 96 h / >= 0.95 from 168 h January; passive at chance). It left three things open, which this addendum closes as far as simulation can: (A) is the campaign robust to a smaller swing, to setpoint overrides, and to buildings that log fewer
signals? (B) if the campaign is wired into the engine as a test, do the resulting `vav_zone` verdicts pass a stated honesty-and-resolution bar? Addendum 14 found the passive engine answers 0 of 128 `vav_zone` episodes, so any resolution here is new; the substantive
questions are honesty and cost. Nothing here touches SEALED data, T1-T6 suites or real data. No frozen `active/*.py` file is modified: the wiring lives in a new module `dr/campaign_eval.py`.

**Data (fresh; never scored).** New EnergyPlus campaigns (`scripts/48`, physics venv) with code seeds 4, 5, 6 -- Addendum 13 used 1-3 and the design-shaping check used 0 -- for the same 7 train-role topologies (`sys07_vav_reheat`, `vav_2ahu_2loops`, `vav_3ahu_2loops`, `dev_E`-`dev_H`;
`sys03_psz_ac` has no VAV campaign), both seasons (July = cooling, January = heating), 2 warm-up days + 12 campaign days, 2-hour blocks, base 24 / 20 C, both setpoints shifted, exactly as Addendum 13, over a grid of half-swing `a` in {0.5, 1.0, 1.5} K x override dropout `p` in {0, 0.25}
(6 configurations x 2 seasons x 7 buildings x 3 seeds = 252 runs). Dropout models a BAS/occupant override: each zone independently, in each 2-hour block, with probability `p`, ignores the campaign and sits at the base setpoint (code 0); the logged setpoint reflects it. The dropout mask is drawn after
the +-1 codes from a separate stream, so the codes themselves are identical to those of a `p = 0` campaign with the same seed.

**Part A -- robustness sweep (model-free; nothing is fitted).** Score exactly as Addendum 13: `S(m, o) = max over the signal set |corr(u_m, signal_o)|` on the first `L` campaign hours, `L` in {24, 48, 96, 168, 288}, `legacy` noise primary (`harsh` reported; reheat energy gets multiplicative noise
sd 0.02 / 0.10 like flow), 20 noise draws, mean over the 3 seeds, rank-1 zone-to-box accuracy (ties as expected value), 7 buildings counted equally, building-cluster bootstrap 95% CI (500). A cell WORKS if mean rank-1 >= 0.90 in BOTH seasons at that `L`. Sweeps: amplitude x dropout with the
`flow + t_dis` signal set; and, at `a = 1.5, p = 0` only, signal sets {`flow + t_dis`, `flow`, `t_dis`, `t_dis + reheat`} (the last standing for a building that logs discharge temperature and a reheat command but no airflow). Frozen readouts (descriptive; no pass/fail): R1 the smallest amplitude
that WORKS at some `L <= 168` h with `p = 0`; R2 whether `a = 1.5, p = 0.25` WORKS by `L <= 168` and by `L <= 288`; R3 which signal sets WORK by `L <= 168`; for every configuration the shortest working `L` (or "not by 288 h") and the swing-hours `a x L` as a crude comfort load.

**Part B -- wiring and end-to-end value on `vav_zone` (`dr/campaign_eval.py`, `scripts/50`).** *Episodes:* the `vav_zone` episodes of `readiness.SimKind.episodes` (the frozen pipeline's construction; 16 per topology, 8 wrong / 8 right) for the 7 campaign topologies = 112 episodes; the 16 `sys03_psz_ac`
episodes have no campaign, are used only as passive-model training data, and are not evaluated. *Stage 1, passive (the frozen engine, unchanged):* leave-one-topology-out over the 8 `EXISTING` topologies; likelihood + calibrated `beta` fitted on the other 7; `active` policy; frozen `sel_slice` fit and run options; tau 0.95; nmax 24; one visit window `h32(ep_id) % 8`;
seed `h32(ep_id) % 10000`; legacy telemetry; **with Addendum 7/10's frozen futility rule (window 4, margin 0.05) and no channel exclusion**. If the belief leaves (0.05, 0.95) the episode is resolved passively and stops. *Stage 2, escalation:* if stage 1 ends unresolved (futile or out of budget) the campaign is run.
The campaign's evidence about zone `m` is `S(m, o)` over all boxes on the first `L` hours, `L` in {96, 168} (both frozen in advance), `flow + t_dis`, `legacy` noise (one draw, seeded by episode and campaign), from the `a = 1.5, p = 0` campaigns of the episode's topology. *Likelihood link:* the campaign multiplies each candidate's
belief by `exp(kappa_L * S(m, box_j))`, i.e. a one-parameter conditional-logit link; `kappa_L` is fitted by maximum likelihood (grid {1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 15, 20, 30, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200}, cross-entropy of the softmax over all boxes) on the TRAINING topologies' campaigns only (the other 6 campaign topologies, both seasons, all 3 seeds, all zones), separately for each `L`. The posterior after
the campaign is `post_j ∝ post_passive_j * exp(kappa_L S_j)`; `P(doc) = post_0`; the verdict rule is the engine's (>= 0.95 CONFIRMED, <= 0.05 REFUTED, else unresolved). *Evaluation unit:* every episode is scored under each of the 6 campaign realisations (2 seasons x seeds 4-6), each (episode, campaign) a row; building-cluster bootstrap over the 7 topologies.
*Policies:* P0 = stage 1 only (today's best passive behaviour); P1 = P0 then a 96 h campaign; P2 = P0 then a 168 h campaign; descriptive comparator C = campaign only from the engine's prior (0.5 documented, the rest equal), for L = 96 and 168.
*Code-path check (must pass before any result is read):* for every topology the campaign's zone-to-box truth equals the pool's `vav_zone` truth for every member. *Metrics per policy (pooled and by season):* resolved fraction; accuracy among resolved (class-balanced over is_wrong / is_right); wrong-and-confident rate over all rows; false-alarm rate (REFUTED among documentation-right rows); missed-fault rate (CONFIRMED among documentation-wrong rows);
localisation (documentation-wrong rows: the most probable owner is the true owner); mean passive reads per episode; campaign hours per row and per correct resolved verdict; and a total-cost table `reads + r x campaign hours` for r in {0, 0.1, 1, 10} (r = a campaign hour in units of one passive read; not priced by us).

**Frozen reading rules (Part B), for each of P1 and P2, pooled over seasons and reported per season:** PASSES if resolved >= 0.70 AND accuracy among resolved >= 0.90 AND false-alarm rate <= 0.10; RESOLVES BUT IS DISHONEST if resolved >= 0.70 and accuracy < 0.90; HONEST BUT PARTIAL if accuracy >= 0.90 and 0.30 <= resolved < 0.70; otherwise FAILS. Overall: wiring
HELPS `vav_zone` (in simulation) if P1 or P2 PASSES pooled and in both seasons; HELPS IN ONE SEASON ONLY if it PASSES pooled but not in both; otherwise it does NOT help. The thresholds were set knowing Addendum 13's numbers (they are the point of this addendum, not blind guesses).

**Limitations named up front.** Simulation only: the same simulator generates the campaigns, the passive pools and the ground truth; one HVAC family, one VAV per zone (a box serving several zones would make rank-1 ties), hourly reporting, July and January only (no shoulder season), no occupants, no actuator saturation
beyond what EnergyPlus models, and `p` is a crude override model (a real override may push the setpoint the wrong way rather than leave it at base). Only 112 episodes (56 wrong-documentation) in 7 buildings, so intervals are wide and the 0.90 / 0.70 lines can be straddled. `kappa_L` rests on 6 training buildings. Sensor-sampling interval, occupancy-restricted dithering and thermal carry-over are not tested (the
simulator has no setback, so any "occupied hours only" result would be misleading). A campaign's cost is reported in hours and swing-hours, never converted to money or technician time here. This tests the identification-and-verdict logic, not a BAS interface, which does not exist; it changes no other slot.

**Result of Part A (2026-09-24, `scripts/48` generated 252 campaigns, 0 failed, ~9 min; `scripts/49` scored them once, 8 s).** Generation-level check before scoring: the logged setpoints span exactly base +- a for a = 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5 K, and with `p = 0.25` 24.2% of zone-blocks sit at the base setpoint
(0% with `p = 0`). Mean zone-to-box rank-1 under `legacy` noise, 7 buildings, [building-cluster 95% CI], lengths 24 / 48 / 96 / 168 / 288 h (chance about 0.10):
*1.5 K, no dropout:* July 0.69 / 0.87 / 0.98 [0.97, 1.00] / 1.00 / 1.00; January 0.83 / 0.91 / 0.92 [0.88, 0.96] / 0.95 / 1.00 -> WORKS at **96 h** (swing-hours 144). *1.5 K, 25% overrides:* July 0.77 / 0.81 / 0.97 / 0.99 / 1.00; January 0.81 / 0.92 / 0.95 / 0.95 / 1.00 -> WORKS at 96 h: overrides cost nothing measurable.
*1.0 K:* July 0.58 / 0.77 / 0.94 / 0.99 / 1.00 but January 0.72 / 0.83 / 0.83 / 0.87 / 0.98 -> works only at **288 h** (with or without overrides). *0.5 K:* July reaches 0.92 and January 0.82 by 288 h -> never works. *Signals (1.5 K, no dropout):* `t_dis` alone reproduces `flow + t_dis` exactly (July 0.98 at 96 h, January 0.93; works at 96 h); `t_dis + reheat` is identical to `t_dis`
(the reheat series is live in every box -- reheat power is simply redundant with discharge temperature here); **`flow` alone does not work**: July 0.91 at 96 h but January 0.46 / 0.55 / 0.61 at 96 / 168 / 288 h (in heating the reheat box holds minimum airflow and moves discharge temperature instead). `harsh` noise gives the same shortest working lengths as `legacy` (96 h, 288 h, none).
Frozen readouts: **R1** smallest half-swing that works by 168 h with no dropout = 1.5 K (at 96 h); **R2** 1.5 K with 25% overrides works by 168 h (yes) and by 288 h (yes, at 96 h); **R3** signal sets that work by 168 h = `flow + t_dis`, `t_dis`, `t_dis + reheat` (not `flow` alone).
*Reading:* in simulation the swing cannot be shrunk below 1.5 K without a 3x longer campaign, and a building must log discharge temperature (or an equivalent); airflow alone fails in winter. Comfort load at the working point: 1.5 K x 96 h = 144 swing-hours.

**Result of Part B (2026-09-24, `scripts/50`, run once, 20 s; the plumbing was smoke-tested first on Addendum 13's already-scored campaigns with only row counts printed -- that smoke test caught and fixed a bug in `fit_kappa`, which had stacked score matrices of buildings with different numbers of boxes and now sums the per-building softmax log-likelihoods; the "unbounded" flag of `fit_kappa` was also redefined from "hit the top of the grid" to "no worse at the top of the grid" before the real run).**
Code-path check passed. Passive stage: 112 episodes, 0 resolved passively, all 112 stopped as futile at a mean of 8.3 reads. Fitted link strength `kappa` = 30 in all 14 fits (7 held-out buildings x 2 lengths), none unbounded. Verdict rows (each episode under 6 campaign realisations):
P0 passive only: resolved 0.00. **P1 (passive, then 96 h): resolved 0.94 [0.92, 0.97] (July 0.96, January 0.93), accuracy among resolved 1.000, wrong-and-confident 0.0%, false alarms 0.0%, missed faults 0.0%, localisation of wrong documents 0.93.
P2 (passive, then 168 h): resolved 0.95 (July 0.99, January 0.91), accuracy 1.000, 0.0% / 0.0% / 0.0%, localisation 0.98.** The campaign-only comparators are indistinguishable from P1 / P2 (0.94 / 0.95 resolved, accuracy 1.000), so the 8.3 passive reads per episode contribute nothing on this slot. Cost per correct resolved verdict (reads + r x campaign hours):
P1 8.8 / 19.0 / 110.4 / 1024.8 and P2 8.7 / 26.4 / 185.1 / 1772.7 for r = 0 / 0.1 / 1 / 10; about 102 (P1) and 176 (P2) campaign hours per correct verdict. **Frozen verdicts: P1 PASSES (pooled, July, January), P2 PASSES (pooled, July, January); overall: wiring HELPS `vav_zone` (in simulation).**
*Caveats that bear on how much to read into it.* (1) Zero errors and 100% accuracy is the result of a very clean simulator, not a demonstration that the link is exact: the 672 rows are 112 episodes x 6 realisations, so the effective sample is closer to 112 episodes -- a rule-of-three bound puts the true wrong-confident rate at up to ~3% -- and the building-cluster
interval [1.00, 1.00] is degenerate (no errors to resample), not proof of certainty. The unresolved 5-9% are the zones whose top box narrowly wins (Addendum 13's rank-1 misses), which the calibrated link declines to call. (2) "Campaign hours per verdict" charges the whole campaign to every episode; one campaign dithers all zones at once and identifies all of them, so per-zone cost is lower when a whole building is checked, but a single-zone
campaign has a different comfort footprint -- not priced here. (3) Passive stage and campaign were both drawn from the same simulator; the campaign in Part B had no overrides (Part A shows 25% overrides do not matter at 1.5 K). (4) The wiring is a new module and an evaluation script (`dr/campaign_eval.py`, `scripts/50`); `diagnose_connection`, the engine and any BAS interface are unchanged, and `kappa` would need
recalibration on real campaigns. (5) All of this is one HVAC family with one VAV per zone.

## Addendum 16 (dated 2026-09-24, written BEFORE generating or scoring the evaluation runs): how reliable is a technician-style single-box override test, and what are its measured rates?

**Motivation (discussed with the user).** Addendum 15's campaign needs days of setpoint dithering and write access to the building -- not something a technician does while testing. The standard technique is short and per box: command ONE box to a distinctive state through the control system
and watch which zone responds. The engine already models this as `active.physical.ACTUATE_OBSERVE` (a Bernoulli test on a candidate owner: "does the member respond?"), but its true-positive rate 0.95, false-positive rate 0.05 and cost 6 are ASSUMPTIONS, never measured (the module says so).
This addendum measures how reliably the test works in simulation and replaces the assumed rates with measured ones. It does not modify any frozen `active/*.py` file: it re-uses `physical_eig` / `update_posterior` from `active/physical.py` unchanged.

**Mechanism (simulation; `interventions.run_override_tests`, physics venv).** All seven train-role VAV buildings use VAV-reheat boxes with hot-water reheat coils and a 10-minute timestep. One EnergyPlus run holds one override test of EVERY box: 2 undisturbed days, then one box per day (seeded random order, seeded start hour in {9, 11, 13, 15}), each
override lasting 2 hours, zone temperatures reported every 10 minutes (BAS-style trend). Three commands: `full_cool` (the box's minimum-airflow fraction forced to 1.0 = full airflow, and its reheat coil switched off -- the zone is driven to maximum cooling in every season), `close` (the box's availability schedule set to 0 = damper shut, zero airflow), and
`flow_only` (airflow forced to maximum, reheat untouched). July (cooling) and January (heating), 7 buildings (`sys07_vav_reheat`, `vav_2ahu_2loops`, `vav_3ahu_2loops`, `dev_E`-`dev_H`), test-order seeds 1, 2, 3 (126 runs).

**Disclosure of design-shaping probes (made BEFORE this addendum; NOT part of the evaluation).** Seed 0 on `sys07_vav_reheat`, July, noise-free, all three commands. It shaped four things. (1) *Alignment:* the simulator applies a schedule's hour value one hour early (the same shift Addendum 13 found for setpoints; confirmed here from the logged box flow, which is zero in
hourly indices [h0 - 1, h0 + 1) for a `close` test starting at clock hour h0), so a test labelled to start at h0 actually runs over [h0 - 1, h0 + 1); my first scoring used the label and compared the second override hour with the first, which looked like a "zone-specific time-of-day confound". (2) I therefore first added a history-corrected statistic (each zone's own change compared with its typical
change at that clock time on the other days); once the alignment was fixed the simpler peers-only statistic worked as well or better, so **peers-only is the PRIMARY statistic and the history-corrected one is reported as a secondary robustness check**. (3) The probe showed `full_cool` and `close` responding clearly and `flow_only` weakly, which is why all three commands are evaluated. (4) Zero-flow timing showed the window is 120 min, so response windows up to 120 min lie fully inside it.
Probe accuracy numbers were used for nothing else: durations, thresholds and rules below are fixed a priori and seed 0 is excluded from every evaluation.

**Part A -- measured operating characteristics (model-free).** For test `k` on day `d` (actual start `s = (24 d + h0 - 1)` hours), zone `z`'s score is `S_z = -(dT_z - median_z dT)` for `full_cool` / `flow_only` (extra COOLING relative to peers) and `+(dT_z - median_z dT)` for `close` (extra WARMING), where `dT_z` = mean zone temperature over the first `D` minutes of the
override minus the mean over the 60 minutes before it, and the median runs over all zones of the building (building-wide drift is common mode). `D` in {30, 60, 120} min. Zone-temperature noise added per sample: `none`, `legacy` (sd 0.25 K, as the project's discharge-temperature noise), `harsh` (0.5 K); 20 draws per run. Metrics per command, season, `D`, noise: top-1 identification (the highest-scoring zone is the zone the tested box serves; ties as
expected value), AUROC (served zone against the other zones), and for thresholds `theta` in {0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0} K the true-positive rate `P(S_served >= theta)` and false-positive rate `P(S_other >= theta)`; mean over the 3 seeds, 7 buildings counted equally, building-cluster bootstrap 95% CI (500). *Measured rates (leave-one-building-out):* for each held-out building `theta` is the smallest grid value whose FPR on the other 6 buildings' tests is <= 0.05
(else the largest), and the TPR / FPR at that `theta` are then measured on the held-out building. Frozen readouts: **A1** for each command the shortest `D` whose top-1 is >= 0.90 in BOTH seasons (`legacy`); **A2** at `D = 60`, `legacy`: the measured TPR and FPR per season against the assumed 0.95 / 0.05 -- "assumed rates held" if TPR >= 0.95 and FPR <= 0.05 in both seasons, otherwise "assumed rates optimistic" (or "pessimistic" if both are better by a
margin of 0.02); **A3** peers-only versus history-corrected top-1; **A4** `harsh` versus `legacy`. Code-path checks (must pass before any result is read): 10-minute series present with 144 steps per day; for every `close` run the box flow is zero in exactly the two hours of the shifted window and non-zero in the hour before and after; the run's zone-to-box truth equals the pool's `vav_zone` truth.

**Part B -- end to end on `vav_zone` (`scripts/53`).** The same 112 episodes as Addendum 15 (7 buildings, 56 wrong / 56 right documentation; each episode under every (season, seed) run = 672 rows per policy). Stage 1 is Addendum 15's frozen passive engine (futility on; 0 of 112 resolved there). Stage 2 escalates to technician-style tests: candidate `j` (a box) is commanded (`full_cool` or `close`), the outcome is
`yes` if the episode's zone scores `S >= theta` in that box's simulated test (`D = 60`, `legacy` noise, one noise draw per test), and the belief is updated by the engine's own Bernoulli rule (`update_posterior`) with the measured rates (Laplace-smoothed `(k + 1) / (n + 2)` from the 6 training buildings, same command, season, `D`, noise), `theta` chosen on the training buildings as above. The next candidate is chosen by `physical_eig` on
the current belief (each candidate at most 3 times; a repeat uses a different seed's run and an independent noise draw); testing stops at P(doc) >= 0.95 (CONFIRMED) or <= 0.05 (REFUTED) or after 4 tests. Policies: `T_cool`, `T_close` (measured rates) and, with the same outcomes but the engine's assumed rates 0.95 / 0.05 in the update and selection, `T_cool_assumed`, `T_close_assumed`. Metrics: as Addendum 15 (resolved fraction,
class-balanced accuracy among resolved, wrong-and-confident, false alarms, missed faults) plus tests per episode and forced-operation minutes per episode and per correct verdict; building-cluster bootstrap. **Frozen reading rules:** each policy is judged by Addendum 15's rule (PASSES if resolved >= 0.70, accuracy among resolved >= 0.90 and false-alarm rate <= 0.10; RESOLVES BUT IS DISHONEST; HONEST BUT PARTIAL; FAILS), pooled and per season. Overall: the technician-style
override WORKS (in simulation) if `T_cool` or `T_close` PASSES pooled and in both seasons; it works in one season only if one passes pooled but not both. The assumed-rate policies are descriptive: they show how much the invented 0.95 / 0.05 rates change resolution and honesty.

**Limitations named up front.** Simulation only, one simulated HVAC family (VAV-reheat boxes with water coils, one box per zone), the two seasons July and January, hourly-and-10-minute output, occupied-hour tests, a 2-hour override and no occupants; the zone response is a clean physical signal plus iid sensor noise, and real zones have unmodelled disturbances (doors, occupancy, solar transients) that make the false-positive rate higher. `flow_only` is a weak command by design. Localisation (which box serves a zone that a test refuted) is not evaluated:
a refuted documented link tells you the tested box's real zone, not the refuted zone's real box. Cost is reported in tests and forced-operation minutes, never converted to money or technician time here. No BAS interface exists; this tests the identification logic only.

**Deviation 1 to Addendum 16 (2026-09-24; logged BEFORE any Part A or Part B result was read).** The pre-registered code-path check ("for every `close` run the box flow is zero in exactly the two hours of the shifted window") FAILED on its first run: all 180 January `close` tests (0 of 180 July tests) had zero flow one hour LATER
than the July-derived window [h0 - 1, h0 + 1); the July tests all passed. Diagnosis (box flow only; no identification statistic computed): the schedule shift is not a constant -1 h -- it is -1 h in July and 0 h in January, which is consistent with daylight saving (the July run period is inside the simulator's daylight-saving window, the January one is not; that cause was not separately verified).
The design-shaping probe was July-only and could not reveal it. Consequence: `override_eval.SCHEDULE_SHIFT_H` is now per season ({july: -1, january: 0}) and `actual_start_hour(start_hour, season)` / `test_scores(..., season=...)` take the season; the frozen check is now "the box flow is zero in exactly the two hours of THE SEASON'S shifted window and non-zero just before and just after", applied to every `close` test
in both seasons. No duration, threshold, statistic or reading rule was changed. Because the July shift was already known (Addendum 13), the same correction applies to every July window in the design above; the January windows start on their label.

**Result of Part A (2026-09-24; 126 runs generated by `scripts/51`, all clean, ~4 min; `scripts/52` scored them once after Deviation 1, 11 s).** Code-path checks passed on all 126 runs (series length; season-shifted zero-flow window for every `close` test; run truth == pool truth). Top-1 identification of the served zone under `legacy` noise, 7 buildings, [building-cluster 95% CI], D = 30 / 60 / 120 min:
**`full_cool`** July 0.98 / 0.99 / 0.99, January 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00, AUROC 1.00 (`harsh` noise 0.99 in both seasons at D = 60). **`close`** July 0.93 / 0.95 / 0.95 [0.90, 0.99 at D = 60], January 0.59 / 0.61 / 0.63 [0.37, 0.86]. **`flow_only`** July 0.80 / 0.80 / 0.81, January 0.50 / 0.51 / 0.51 (chance about 0.10-0.17). The peers-only (primary) and history-corrected (secondary) statistics agree to within 0.01 in every cell (A3), so the simpler one is enough.
*Measured rates* (D = 60, `legacy`, leave-one-building-out `theta`; held-out TPR / FPR): `full_cool` theta 0.64 K, TPR 0.99 (July) / 0.98 (January), FPR 0.03 / 0.03; `close` theta 1.29 / 1.00 K, TPR 0.90 / 0.57, FPR 0.03 / 0.03; `flow_only` theta 0.68 / 0.75 K, TPR 0.75 / 0.44, FPR 0.03 / 0.02. **Frozen readouts:** A1 shortest D with top-1 >= 0.90 in both seasons: `full_cool` 30 min; `close` and `flow_only` not by 120 min; A2 the engine's assumed 0.95 / 0.05 HELD for `full_cool` and are OPTIMISTIC for `close` and `flow_only` (their true-positive rates are far lower, especially in January); A4 `harsh` noise gives the same shortest working D as `legacy` for every command.

**Result of Part B (2026-09-24, `scripts/53`, run once after a plumbing smoke test that printed only row counts, 20 s).** Passive stage: 0 of 112 resolved passively (mean 8.3 reads). Training-measured rates used: `full_cool` TPR 0.986 / FPR 0.031 (July), 0.986 / 0.035 (January); `close` 0.884 / 0.026 (July), 0.511 / 0.042 (January). Per policy, pooled (July / January) -- resolved, accuracy among resolved, wrong-and-confident, false alarms, missed faults, tests per row, forced minutes per row:
**`T_cool` (measured rates): 1.00 (0.99 / 1.00), 0.946 [0.87, 0.99] (0.946 / 0.946), 5.4%, 8.3% (8.9% / 7.7%), 2.4%, 1.23 tests, 74 min -> PASSES pooled, July and January.** `T_cool_assumed` (engine's 0.95 / 0.05): 0.99, 0.947, 5.2%, 9.8% (July 8.3%, January 11.3%), 0.6%, 1.54 tests, 93 min -> pooled PASSES, January FAILS on false alarms.
`T_close` (measured): 0.67 (July 0.95, January 0.40), 0.940, 4.5%, 7.7% (July 15.5%, January 0.0%), 1.2%, 2.58 tests, 155 min -> pooled HONEST BUT PARTIAL, July FAILS (false alarms 15.5% > 10%), January HONEST BUT PARTIAL. `T_close_assumed`: 0.98, 0.824, 16.8%, 33.6%, 0.0%, 1.51 tests -> RESOLVES BUT IS DISHONEST in both seasons.
**Frozen verdict: the technician-style override WORKS (in simulation)** -- through the `full_cool` command; `close` does not meet the bar and `flow_only` was only evaluated in Part A.
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (1) `full_cool` passes but is not the near-perfect result of the campaign: 5.4% wrong-and-confident and 8.3% false alarms sit close to the pre-registered 10% false-alarm line, and accuracy among resolved (0.946) is right at the stated 0.95, not above it. A check of where the errors sit (post hoc, descriptive): the 28 false-alarm rows come from 14 of 56 right-documentation episodes (six episodes fail in 3-4 of their 6 realisations, i.e. a zone whose response is weak;
the rest fail once) and the 8 missed-fault rows are 8 different episodes failing once -- so the errors are partly systematic (weak-responding zones, which a repeat test on the same physics does not cure) and partly noise. (2) The measured rates differ from the assumed ones in the direction that matters: the assumed 0.95 / 0.05 are about right for `full_cool` (measured 0.986 / 0.03, which lets one test suffice -- 1.23 tests and 74 forced minutes against 1.54 tests and 93 minutes with the assumed rates) but badly
optimistic for `close`, where the invented TPR of 0.95 against a measured 0.88 (July) / 0.51 (January) turns a weak test into 34% false alarms. `ACTUATE_OBSERVE`'s description ("command the candidate's damper/reheat/valve to a distinctive state") does not say WHICH state; the simulation says it matters a great deal. (3) A test verifies or refutes the documented link (about 74 forced minutes, 1.2 tests, per zone); it does not localise the true box of a refuted zone (Addendum 15's campaign does, for all zones at once, in days). (4) Simulation only: a clean zone-temperature response plus iid sensor noise, one HVAC family, one box per zone, no occupants or unmodelled disturbances, so the real false-positive rate would be higher.
(5) Consequence flagged, not acted on: `active/measurement.diagnose_connection` already recommends a physical test through `best_physical_test` using the assumed rates; replacing them with the measured `full_cool` rates and naming the command ("drive the box to full cooling: damper maximum, reheat off, watch zone temperatures for about an hour") would need an edit to the frozen `active/physical.py` and needs the user's approval.

## Addendum 17 (dated 2026-09-24, written BEFORE any `vav_zone` evidence statistic has been computed on Mortar data): does the passive `vav_zone` limit of Addendum 11 hold on real buildings?

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** Addendum 11 found, in simulation only, that the passive `vav_zone` channels cannot tell which box serves a zone even with noise-free, fully instrumented data (window rank-1
0.143 vs chance 0.123; AUROC 0.600 / 0.523 / 0.437 on ch0 / ch1 / ch2), and Addenda 13, 15 and 16 (perturbation and override tests) are built on that premise. The only real evidence so far is one building (ORNL, 10 rooms, no box airflow: ch1
weak-but-real, ch0 AUROC 0.513, ch2 0.724 on 10 of 140 units). The Mortar timeseries (`data_cache/timeseries/mortar/`, licence status in `data_cache/timeseries/LICENSES.md`: creator's email "fairly certain" BSD-3-Clause) and the intake converter
(`docmismatch/mortar_adapter.py`, `scripts/56`) now give real multi-box buildings. Readiness (`results/docmismatch/intake/mortar_readiness.md`) found exactly two Mortar buildings whose recorded points support all three `vav_zone` channels for >= 96% of
their documented connections: `bldg8` (24 boxes) and `bldg30` (99 boxes). This addendum tests the passive-identification question on them, model-free (no likelihood, no engine, no reliability layer, nothing fitted).

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) The readiness table: which points exist and how well they cover the exported windows -- no relationship between streams. (2) A LABELS-ONLY check that the truth-audit rule below parses the point labels of
the four candidate buildings: `bldg8` 24/24 claims agree, `bldg1` 8/8, `bldg27` 17/17, `bldg30` 96/99 with 3 unparsable -- the three are ids containing letters (`2-4-17A`, `2-5-17B`, `2-B-1`), so pattern B below was widened from digits-only to alphanumeric BEFORE
this addendum (no label disagrees). (3) The converter's unit-plausibility check looked at each stream's median (temperatures) or 90th percentile (flows) to catch wrong units; among the four candidate buildings it dropped only `bldg30`'s `VAV_2-4-24` flow (90th percentile 1.97 "cfm") and found no all-zero stream. (4) The converter chose the more specific
`Supply_Air_Flow_Sensor` over the generic `Air_Flow_Sensor` where a VAV had both (`bldg1`: 8 boxes). No correlation, error channel, rank or accuracy has been computed on any Mortar stream.

**Frozen data.** The exports written by `scripts/56` on 2026-09-24 (converter and windows fixed before this addendum; two 42-day windows per building chosen by data coverage only): `bldg8` 2017-01-15 (coverage 100%) and 2015-07-15 (100%); `bldg30` 2017-01-15 (99%) and 2017-07-15 (86%);
secondary `bldg1` 2017-01-15 (100%) and 2016-07-15 (93%); `bldg27` 2017-01-15 (100%) and 2016-07-15 (96%). sha256 prefixes (`data_cache/intake/mortar/<bldg>/`): `bldg8` trends d959b488ad896815, points a58e1fb140d12ea0, connections edc108aaf416696f; `bldg30` trends 1172ce4d85e26df4,
points 546cfe98dba9549c, connections 47097e7b9b38a5e3; `bldg1` trends bfa1aea995226240, points 26d274a3771bc3a2, connections 80be71bab1d981c2; `bldg27` trends 282b4e4465c447b3, points bf10d1875cb5fc94, connections 6b5d0798361415ed. Hourly grid (UTC clock, US customary
units assumed and range-checked, one-minute data averaged to 15 minutes before the intake's hourly averaging). Primary buildings: `bldg8`, `bldg30`. Secondary (descriptive, channel 0 only, no verdict): `bldg1`, `bldg27`. Not analysed: `bldg40` and `bldg15` (readiness: NOT TESTABLE; running only
their complete subsets would be an analysis choice made after seeing readiness) and every other Mortar building. Not used: SEALED data, T1-T6 suites, any simulated data (except the code-path gate below).

**Truth and its audit.** Truth = the documented Brick VAV -> zone assignment (`connections.csv`, source "Brick model (Mortar)", NOT field-verified; the creator notes the graphs are old and due a refresh). A zone's temperature stream is the sensor attached to the zone or to the VAV that
feeds it, so a zone's identity comes from the same documentation; the test therefore asks whether VAV `v`'s own signals match the temperature stream the documentation ties to `v`, against every other VAV's. Label audit (labels only): the box token of a claim's three labels (VAV flow, VAV discharge temperature, zone temperature) is
pattern A `^[^.]+\.ZONE\.[^.]+\.([^.]+)\.` (e.g. `bldg8.ZONE.AHU01.RM100B.Zone Air Temp` -> `RM100B`) else pattern B `^([0-9A-Za-z]+-[0-9A-Za-z]+-[0-9A-Za-z]+)_` (e.g. `2-4-17A_SpaceTemp` -> `2-4-17A`); a claim PASSES when the three tokens exist and are identical, and is otherwise excluded.

**Analysis set (frozen).** The documented (VAV, zone) pairs that pass the audit AND whose VAV flow, VAV discharge temperature and zone temperature series are all present with >= 50% hourly coverage over the days with data and varying beyond the engine's assumed sensor noise
(`intake_report._series_status == "ok"`, i.e. not flat, not sparse). Owners (candidates) and members are both restricted to this set, so every candidate has all three channels. It must hold >= 8 pairs per primary building, else that building gets no verdict.

**Evidence (identical channels to Addendum 11).** `field_pool.build_field_pool` with three additions made before running (defaults unchanged, tested): `slice_days="all"` (every day of each window that has data, instead of the engine's six positions), `mask_uninformative=False` (raw channel values, as in Addendum 11),
`require_oat=False` (no outdoor-temperature series needed: `bldg30` has none; the context column is a placeholder that this model-free statistic never reads). `E[s, k, o, m]` for channels 0-2 = `evidence.pair_errors` of `vav_zone` exactly as in simulation (fleet common-mode from every VAV / zone that has the series).

**Primary statistic (frozen).** For each primary building: rank-1 identification accuracy `acc` = `diagnosability.rank1_accuracy(E, truth, cands, group, (0, 1, 2), pooled=True)` over every day-slice of both windows on the analysis set (per member: each channel's mean tf(e) across the slices, standardised across the candidate boxes, averaged over the three
channels, lowest wins, ties count 1/#tied), with `chance` = mean 1/|candidates|. Null: a permutation test on the same score matrix -- 10,000 random one-to-one re-assignments of which box serves which zone (seed 0); `p = (1 + #{acc_perm >= acc}) / 10001`.

**Reported without rules (all buildings where computable).** The window-scale statistic comparable to Addendum 11 (`slice_days="engine"`, window-aggregated, mean over the two windows); ch0 / ch1 / ch2 / all pairs of channels separately; per-channel AUROC and d' (`diagnosability.class_rows`) for comparison with 0.600 / 0.523 / 0.437; top-3 accuracy (the true box among the three lowest-error candidates: what a technician would need to check);
each window separately; accuracy against record length (first 3, 7, 14, 28, 42 days of each window, pooled); secondary buildings channel 0 with the same permutation p.

**Gates (all must pass before any accuracy is read; a failed gate means NO verdict, reported as such).** G1 code path: the score-matrix implementation used for the permutation test reproduces `rank1_accuracy` to 1e-12 on the real `E`. G2 the analysis set has >= 8 pairs in each primary building. G3 sensitivity: coupling injected into the real data must be recovered -- for each zone the hourly temperature
series becomes `temp + alpha * sd(temp) * (z(flow_v) + z(t_dis_v)) / sqrt(2)` with `z` the standardised hourly series of its own box over the whole export (in memory; files untouched), and at `alpha = 1.0` the primary statistic must reach `acc >= 0.80` in both primary buildings (otherwise the pipeline cannot see even a strong coupling and the result is INCONCLUSIVE). The grid `alpha` in {0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0} is reported as the
minimum detectable coupling for each building.

**Frozen reading rules (written before running).** With `acc` and `p` per primary building, after the gates: **PASSIVE `vav_zone` IDENTIFIES ON REAL DATA** if `acc >= 0.50` and `p < 0.01` in BOTH; **AT CHANCE -- ADDENDUM 11 HOLDS ON REAL DATA** if `p >= 0.05` in BOTH; otherwise **BUILDING-DEPENDENT OR WEAK** (report each building's `acc`, `p`, and the top-3 accuracy). An AT CHANCE
verdict is always reported together with the minimum detectable coupling from G3, because a null on a noisy or short record is not evidence that no coupling exists.

**Implementation to be written and tested BEFORE the run, then frozen** (a deviation is logged, never silently fixed): the three `build_field_pool` options (existing tests must pass unchanged); `dr/realdata_diag.py` (label audit, analysis set, score matrix, permutation p, top-k, coupling injection, verdict rules); `scripts/57_real_vavzone_passive.py`; tests for each, including that the audit rule reproduces the labels-only counts above (with the widened pattern B, `bldg30` 99/99 pass).

**Limitations named up front.** (1) The truth is documentation (BAS point naming), not field verification, and the buildings contain no known documentation errors: this measures IDENTIFIABILITY, not error detection. An at-chance result cannot separate "the channels cannot identify it" from "some documented assignments are wrong"; the audit only removes claims whose own labels disagree. (2) Two primary buildings, both anonymised
and from one dataset; the p-value is per building and the verdict needs both. (3) Hourly resampling of 15-minute (or one-minute) data hides sub-hourly dynamics a finer analysis might use; not tested here. (4) UTC clock and unknown local time do not matter for cross-box statistics but do for any diurnal alignment. (5) Real zones are subject to occupancy, solar and schedule differences that the simulation lacks: that is exactly what is being tested, but it also means a positive result could come from
shared schedules between neighbouring boxes rather than from the physical serving relationship; the exact-match rank-1 and top-3 accuracy cannot distinguish these. (6) A positive result would NOT show that a wrong documented assignment would be caught: it would need verified-wrong cases. (7) The licence for the Mortar timeseries rests on the creator's email; nothing derived here is to be redistributed before a definitive confirmation.

**Result of Addendum 17 (2026-09-24; `scripts/57_real_vavzone_passive.py` run once as pre-registered, 37 s, after a plumbing smoke test on a SYNTHETIC world only that read no Mortar data; 51 tests pass for the code it uses; report `results/docmismatch/intake/vavzone_passive_report.md`).
Gates: G1 (code path) passed, G2 (>= 8 pairs) passed, G3 (injected coupling alpha = 1.0 recovered, acc >= 0.80) passed in both primary buildings (bldg8 0.875, bldg30 0.884). Frozen verdict: BUILDING-DEPENDENT OR WEAK.**
*Primary statistic (all three channels, full record).* `bldg8` (24 pairs, none excluded): rank-1 **0.167** vs chance 0.042, permutation p **0.0134** (null mean 0.041, 95th percentile 0.125), top-3 0.250 (chance 0.125). `bldg30` (95 pairs): rank-1 **0.042** vs chance 0.011,
p **0.0123** (null mean 0.011, 95th percentile 0.032), top-3 0.126 (chance 0.032). Neither reaches the identification bar (acc >= 0.50) and neither is "at chance" (p >= 0.05 in both is required for that): both sit at about four times chance with p just above 0.01.
*Sensitivity (injected coupling; rank-1):* `bldg8` alpha 0.1 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 1.0 = 0.125 (p 0.065) / 0.375 / 0.500 / 0.875; `bldg30` 0.137 / 0.484 / 0.779 / 0.884. Minimum detectable coupling on the grid = 0.5 (the identification bar) in both buildings.
*Reported without rules.* Per channel (permutation p, 2,000): `bldg8` ch0 0.042 (p 0.66), **ch1 0.250 (p 0.0005)**, ch2 0.042 (p 0.66); `bldg30` ch0 0.000 (p 1.0), **ch1 0.116 (p 0.0005)**, ch2 0.000 (p 1.0); every channel subset that contains ch1 without ch0 is significant, and adding ch0 lowers it. Per-channel AUROC (simulation truth 0.600 / 0.523 / 0.437):
`bldg8` 0.475 / 0.542 / 0.492, `bldg30` 0.462 / 0.553 / 0.530. Visit-scale (engine slice positions, window-aggregated): `bldg8` 0.062 vs 0.042 (16 slices, 48 units), `bldg30` 0.045 vs 0.011 (18 slices, 179 units). Per window (chronological): `bldg8` July 2015 0.000, January 2017 0.208; `bldg30` January 2017 0.042, July 2017 0.012. Record length (first L = 3 / 7 / 14 / 28 / 42 days of each window): `bldg8` 0.125 / 0.125 / 0.167 / 0.167 / 0.167 (p reaches 0.012-0.018 from L = 14);
`bldg30` 0.042 / 0.032 / 0.032 / 0.042 / 0.042 (p 0.011-0.067). Secondary, channel 0 only: `bldg1` (8 pairs) 0.125 vs chance 0.125 (p 0.65); `bldg27` (17 pairs) 0.235 vs 0.059 (p 0.0135, top-3 0.353).
*Clarifications made while running (no rule, threshold or statistic was changed):* (1) `bldg30`'s analysis set is 95, not 98-99: one claim failed the audit because its VAV (`VAV_2-4-24`) has no flow point after the converter dropped it as implausible, and three failed the series check (two zone-temperature streams and one VAV flow with < 50% coverage); the labels-only 99/99 count referred to the graph, before the export. (2) For the secondary buildings, which have no discharge temperature, the analysis set and audit use the channel-0 inputs (VAV flow and zone temperature), the natural reading of "channel 0 only".
(3) The engine-position rule gave 16 and 18 slices in two windows (not 6 per window) because the second window straddles the 1008-hour grid; this only affects the descriptive visit-scale row.
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (a) **Addendum 11's null does not transfer exactly to real data, but its practical conclusion does.** On real buildings the passive `vav_zone` channels carry a small, statistically detectable signal (about four times chance in both buildings, consistently), essentially all of it in ch1 (box discharge temperature against zone temperature; simulation ch1 AUROC 0.523 vs real 0.542 / 0.553); it is far from usable: rank-1 0.167 / 0.042 against the 0.50 bar, and the true box is in the top three for 25% / 13% of zones.
The flow-based channels (ch0, ch2) show no positive signal, and ch0's AUROC is below 0.5 in both buildings (0.475, 0.462), i.e. the true pair tends to look slightly WORSE than the alternatives. (b) The label "building-dependent" in the frozen rule is a misnomer here: both buildings agree; "weak" is the right word. (c) The sensitivity grid puts the natural coupling these channels can see at about or below an injected coupling of 0.1 standard deviations (injecting 0.1 gives 0.125-0.137, 0.25 already gives 0.375-0.484);
that calibration is approximate, since the injection is an idealised linear coupling to the hourly series. (d) Consequence, not acted on: the premise of Addenda 13, 15 and 16 (passive `vav_zone` verification is not usable, so a deliberate test is needed) is supported on two real buildings; it does not need a new statistic to be true, but see the exploratory observations.
*Exploratory observations, NOT pre-registered, each needing its own pre-registered follow-up before anything is adopted:* (e) ch0 correlates flow with the zone-temperature residual assuming a POSITIVE sign; in cooling a box's flow and its zone temperature move in opposite directions, so a sign-agnostic ch0 (as ch1 and ch2 already are) might carry the signal that ch0 currently cancels; untested. (f) ch1 alone reaches 0.250 and 0.116 and is the only channel with signal: whether a ch1-weighted or two-channel statistic would generalise (or is an artefact of choosing after seeing) is unknown; the pre-registered all-three statistic is the result. (g) `bldg8`'s two windows differ (0.000 vs 0.208) and `bldg30`'s point the other way; two windows per building cannot separate season from chance.
*Not concluded:* that no passive statistic could identify `vav_zone` on real data; that the documented assignments are correct (the truth is documentation); anything about error detection (no documented error is present in these buildings); anything about buildings beyond these two.

**Erratum to Addendum 17 and descriptive census note (2026-09-24; found while running the instrumentation census; no rule, threshold or result of Addendum 17 changes).**
(1) *Converter gap.* `mortar_adapter.parse_brick` recognised air handlers only under the Brick class `Air_Handler_Unit`; `bldg30` (and its duplicate `smc`) file its two air handlers under the alias class `AHU`, so the frozen `bldg30` export and its readiness row ("`ahu_vav` not documented") missed its 99 documented AHU -> VAV connections. The correct entry is: 99 documented `ahu_vav` pairs, untestable (no VAV inlet temperature and no AHU airflow are recorded). The converter now accepts `AHU` (tested). Re-exporting all six buildings to a scratch directory changes ONLY `bldg30`'s `connections.csv`
(the 99 claims are added); every other frozen file, including all six `trends.csv` and `points.csv`, is byte-identical, so the frozen exports (hashes above) were kept and NOT regenerated. (2) *The outdoor temperature.* Addendum 17 says `bldg30` "has none". The graph does document outdoor-air and supply-air points on `AHU1` / `AHU2`, but their six streams (with each air handler's speed command) are exactly the six referenced streams missing from the Hugging Face repo, so no outdoor-temperature series is available for `bldg30`; the sentence is true of the data, not of the graph.
(3) *Effect on the result: none.* The primary statistic reads only VAV flow, VAV discharge temperature and zone temperature; the AHU and the outdoor series enter neither the channels, the analysis set nor the audit.
*Instrumentation census (`scripts/59_instrumentation_census.py`, `results/docmismatch/intake/mortar_census.md`; metadata only, no timeseries read; descriptive, no verdict).* Over the 44 distinct Mortar Brick models (a point counts as RECORDED when it links to a timeseries; a channel is SUPPORTED in a building when >= 80% of its documented pairs have every point it reads, the readiness rule): no graph has a water loop, branch, valve or pump node, so `loop_branch`, `branch_coil`, `loop_pump` and `loop_source` cannot be tested on this dataset; `coil_ahu` has no documented pair and `coil_vav` one building (99 reheat coils) with no coil power recorded.
`ahu_vav`: 28 buildings document 2,143 pairs and NONE supports any channel, even if every documented point were trended (no box inlet temperature is documented anywhere; air-handler supply airflow is recorded in 3 buildings). `vav_zone`: 28 buildings, 2,136 pairs; 8 support at least one channel (10 if every documented point were trended), 3 support all three (`bldg1`, `bldg8`, `bldg30`; `bldg1`'s discharge temperature was later found flat in the data). Recorded measurements: zone temperature 20 buildings, VAV airflow 12 (4 at >= 80% of boxes), VAV discharge temperature 12 (7); the mixed-air temperature is documented in 30 buildings and recorded in none; coil power and air-handler heating / cooling meters exist nowhere.
Recorded but unused by our channels: VAV zone temperature setpoints (15 buildings, 1,080 points), AHU supply-air temperature setpoints (25), AHU return-air temperature (23), AHU static pressure (13), VAV / AHU commands. *Reading, with the caveat it needs:* on this corpus only the `vav_zone` connection type -- the one Addendum 17 shows is not passively identifiable -- is testable at all, and the simulation's strongly diagnosable types (`ahu_vav`, `branch_coil`, `loop_branch`, `coil_ahu`) are not testable in any building. The census describes what Mortar's semantic models expose (what was mapped into Brick for its own analytics), which is a LOWER BOUND on what these control systems record; it says nothing about buildings outside this corpus.

## Addendum 18 (dated 2026-09-25, written BEFORE any signal-removal or substitute statistic has been computed): what does a missing signal cost, and can a recorded signal or a technician's reading replace it?

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** The instrumentation census (note after Addendum 17) found that on the Mortar corpus the connection types the simulation diagnoses best (`ahu_vav`, `branch_coil`, `loop_branch`, `coil_ahu`) are untestable from recorded data, because the signals their strongest channels read (box inlet temperature, coil power, water flows, air-handler airflow, mixed-air temperature) are almost never recorded, while `vav_zone`, which the recorded signals do support, is the type Addenda 11 and 17 show is not passively identifiable. Two questions follow. (1) Which single missing signals cost the most? (2) For the cheapest-looking case, `ahu_vav` -- its strongest channel needs one rare signal (box inlet temperature) next to the common air-handler supply temperature -- can a recorded signal (box discharge temperature) or a few technician readings of the inlet temperature stand in for it? The user added the design principle: a missing signal is a price, not a fixed loss. The technician can be asked for an observation that is in no document whenever that costs less than the tests it saves; looking for something undocumented can itself take longer, so the price must be reported, not assumed.

**Three design rules, fixed with the user before writing.** (i) An asked-for observation is counted as a test (the engine's own `n_read` is "the number this whole mechanism is trying to shrink"), weighted by an explicit price. (ii) Accuracy is held fixed as well as effort: a count of tests is trivially preserved by resolving less or by being wrong more. (iii) The price of an observation is not assumed; the study reports the BREAK-EVEN price (the most an observation can cost, in units of one trend read, before asking stops paying). The real price comes from technicians (the field-cost interview, not yet done).

**Staging.** Parts A and B are model-free and are pre-registered here (cheap check first: nothing fitted, no engine). Part C, the engine-level test with break-even prices, is outlined below and is NOT pre-registered here: it gets its own addendum before it is run, and only if Part B finds a substitute worth carrying forward, because how a spot reading enters the pool, and whether channel models must be refitted, depend on Part B's outcome and would be guesses now.

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) Addendum 11's `ahu_vav` numbers (`results/docmismatch/dr/diagnosability/diagnosability_report.md`, 6 buildings, `legacy` evidence, window-aggregated rank-1, chance 0.378): ch0 0.969, ch1 0.618, ch2 0.400, all three 0.824. (2) A metadata-only look at the eight `EXISTING` simulators (no statistic computed): six have an `ahu_vav` slot with at least two air handlers -- `sys03_psz_ac` (10 handlers, one box each, no `reheat` series), `vav_2ahu_2loops`, `vav_3ahu_2loops`, `dev_F`, `dev_G`, `dev_H` -- and two have a single handler (`sys07_vav_reheat`, `dev_E`; no alternative candidate, so they contribute nothing). Every simulated box carries `t_in`, `t_dis` and `flow`; all but `sys03_psz_ac` also carry `reheat`; the outdoor series `ENV|oat` exists. (3) Addendum 11 notes that ch0's noise-free error for the true pair is exactly zero: in the simulator a box's inlet temperature IS its handler's supply temperature (no duct gain, no lag), and with the coil off `t_dis` is expected to equal `t_in` (`evidence._vav_pred_dat`; Check C2 measures it). So the simulator is optimistic about any inlet-based statistic, and the oracle proxy V1 below is close to the baseline BY CONSTRUCTION -- that is not a finding. The informative parts are the realistic selection rules (V2, V3), the unfiltered proxy (V4), the spot readings, and the offset sensitivity. No accuracy of any variant, and no signal-removal ceiling beyond the per-slot numbers already in Addendum 11, has been computed.

**Part A -- which single missing signal costs the most (model-free).** Data: the eight `EXISTING` topologies, `legacy` evidence built exactly as in `scripts/43` (`level_evidence(base, "legacy", 0)`, `build_E`, masking off), window-aggregated rank-1 (Addendum 11's primary measure), each building counting equally, building-cluster bootstrap 95% CIs (500 resamples), chance = mean 1/|candidates|. Slots: `ahu_vav`, `loop_branch`, `branch_coil`, `coil_ahu` (`vav_zone` is left out: its ceiling is at chance with every signal present, so no signal can cost it anything). Signal catalogue: `intake_report.CHANNEL_SIGNALS` (which (equipment role, signal) pairs each channel reads; already tested against `evidence.pair_errors`). For a slot and a catalogue signal `s`, `remaining(s)` = the channels that do not read `s`. CEILING(S) of a channel set S = the maximum, over the non-empty subsets of S, of the rank-1 accuracy of the equal-weight standardised average of that subset's channels (Addendum 11's statistic; all 7 subsets of {ch0, ch1, ch2} are computed, not only ch0 / ch1 / ch2 / all3). VALUE(s) = CEILING({ch0, ch1, ch2}) - CEILING(remaining(s)); NO CHANNEL if `remaining(s)` is empty. The ceiling is a maximum over at most 7 subsets on the design data, so it is optimistic, equally on both sides of the difference; it bounds what these channels can do and is not what the engine does (the engine weights channels by fitted likelihoods). Reading rules (descriptive labels; the raw numbers are the result): a signal is CRITICAL for a slot if VALUE >= 0.15 (the threshold of Addendum 11's instrumentation-limited rule) or no channel remains; MINOR if VALUE < 0.05; otherwise MODERATE. The report adds, per signal, how often the Mortar census found it recorded (`mortar_census.md`; descriptive, no rule uses it): the table is meant to show which signals are both critical and rarely recorded, i.e. the candidates for asking a technician.

**Part B -- can a recorded signal or a technician's reading replace `ahu_vav`'s box inlet temperature? (model-free).** Buildings: the six with an `ahu_vav` slot and at least two handlers, each equal weight, building-cluster bootstrap 95% CI (500; with six buildings the intervals are wide and are reported as such; verdicts use point estimates). Evidence: `legacy`, same seeds as Addendum 11. Statistic: ch0's form, the mean over the selected hours of |x_m(h) - sat_o(h)| for box m and candidate handler o, where x_m is the variant's "inlet reading" of box m; the lowest-error candidate is the answer, ties count 1/#tied, chance = mean 1/|candidates|. One visit = one window. For the trended variants the statistic is computed per standard day-slice (a slice is valid when at least 6 of its 24 hours are selected) and averaged over the window's valid slices as `rank1_accuracy` does; a box with no valid slice in a window is skipped and counted. Variants:
- **V0 `inlet_trend`:** x = `t_in`, all hours (the baseline).
- **V1 `dis_reheat_off_oracle`:** x = `t_dis` at the hours when the box's TRUE reheat (noise-free series) is off (|reheat| <= 2% of its maximum, the `_duty` threshold). An upper bound for the proxy, since real buildings do not record reheat state. Undefined for `sys03_psz_ac` (no reheat series), so V1 pools over five buildings.
- **V2 `dis_flow_top50`:** x = `t_dis` at the hours when the box's (noisy) flow is at or above its own median over the window.
- **V3 `dis_flow_top25`:** the same with the box's own 75th percentile. V2 and V3 select hours from the box's own flow only, never from a temperature or from any candidate's series, so the selection cannot favour a candidate (a unit test asserts this).
- **V4 `dis_all`:** x = `t_dis`, all hours (no filtering).
- **S1, S3, S6 `spot_m`:** x = `t_in` at m distinct hours (m = 1, 3, 6) of ONE day, drawn per (box, window, draw): the day uniformly from the window's six standard day-slices, the hours uniformly without replacement from slice hours 8-17 (daytime). Each candidate's supply temperature is the trended `sat` at the same hours. A technician reading carries the same noise as a trended temperature (legacy, sd 0.25 K). **S24 `day_logger`:** all 24 hours of the drawn day (a logger left in for a day). 20 draws per (box, window); no unit is skipped.

Offset sensitivity (the simulator has neither duct gain nor sensor calibration error, which any real inlet estimate has): every variant's x, V0 included, gets a per-box constant offset d_m ~ N(0, s^2) (fresh per box and draw), s in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0} K, 20 draws for s > 0. It stands for duct heat gain and calibration error; it is constant in time, so lags and transient gains are NOT covered. Seeds: one `default_rng` per (building, variant, offset), derived from base seed 18, fixed in code before the run.

Season groups: the verdict uses `SEASON_OF_WINDOW` group 0 (windows 0-1, assumed winter) and group 2 (windows 4-5, assumed summer); groups 1 and 3 are reported without a verdict (Check C4).

**Frozen rule.** A variant WORKS at offset s in season g if rank-1(V) >= 0.80 AND rank-1(V) >= rank-1(V0) - 0.10, both at the same s and g. A season whose V0 at that s is below 0.80 is BASELINE-LIMITED there and counts neither for nor against. The tolerated offset s*(V) is the largest grid value at which V WORKS in every verdict season that is not baseline-limited, and at every smaller grid value. Verdict: USABLE if s* >= 0.5 K; FRAGILE if 0 <= s* < 0.5 K; FAILS if V does not work at s = 0; NO VERDICT if both verdict seasons are baseline-limited at s = 0 or a gate fails. For the spot variants the report also gives the smallest m that WORKS at each s (the price-relevant number: readings per box). The 0.80 bar and the 10-point tolerance are the values discussed with the user; the 0.5 K offset required for USABLE is a judgment about typical duct gain and calibration error, made before running and not derived from data -- the whole grid is reported so a reader can apply another.

**Gates and checks (all computed before any variant is read).** G1 code path: V0 at s = 0 reproduces the cached Addendum-11 `rank1_window["ch0"]` (legacy) for every building to 1e-9 (`results/docmismatch/dr/diagnosability/diagnosability.pkl`), and Part A's `ch0` / `ch1` / `ch2` / `all3` values reproduce the cached ones likewise. G2 null control: with the truth re-assigned by random one-to-one permutations within each candidate set (200 permutations, fixed seed), every variant's pooled rank-1 averages within 0.05 of chance, i.e. neither the truth nor the hour selection leaks into the score; failing means NO VERDICT. C1 the flow rules read no temperature and no candidate series (unit test). C2 the identity the oracle relies on is measured, not assumed: in the noise-free truth, the largest |`t_dis` - `t_in`| over reheat-off hours, per building; if it exceeds 0.05 K the oracle differs from the baseline at s = 0 by that much, which is logged and not corrected. C3 the share of hours and of slices surviving each selection rule, per season group (descriptive). C4 season labels: the window-mean `ENV|oat` of group 0 is lower than that of group 2 in each building; if not, the groups are reassigned by that rule and the change is logged.

**Part C (outline only; NOT pre-registered here).** Engine-level arms on the same simulator, per (slot, signal): A = all signals present (the reference; for `ahu_vav` the plain rule of Addendum 14 gives 59% resolved, accuracy among resolved 1.00, 3.7 reads); B = the signal removed, nothing asked; C = the signal removed and the technician asked for it (readings drawn as in Part B). Metrics per arm: share resolved, accuracy among resolved, effort = sum of price x actions (a trend read = 1; the ask priced p, swept), and the break-even price p* = the largest p at which effort(C) <= effort(B) while C still meets the intended bars (accuracy among resolved >= 0.90 and resolved share within 10 points of arm A). Which other single signals it covers is chosen from Part A's ranking. Part C is written as Addendum 19, before any engine run, ONLY IF Part B finds at least one variant other than V0 and V1 that is USABLE or FRAGILE. If none is, the proxy is dropped and `ahu_vav`'s box inlet temperature has no cheap stand-in in the simulator: the ask, if any, is the inlet temperature itself (S24 is the reference), which Part C would then price directly.

**Limitations named up front.** (1) The simulator is optimistic about inlet-based statistics (adiabatic, instantaneous ducts; the true pair's ch0 error is exactly zero without noise); the constant per-box offset covers only part of what a real building adds. (2) Six buildings, one of which (`sys03_psz_ac`) is ten one-box packaged units; intervals are wide and per-building rows are reported. (3) A ch0-only rank-1 is not the engine's verdict: Part B says whether the signal survives, not how many tests the engine saves (Part C). (4) A technician reading is modeled as noise around the truth; a handheld probe in a ceiling plenum may be worse; the offset grid is the only guard. (5) The price of an observation is not measured here. (6) Two handlers with the same supply-temperature profile cannot be told apart by any temperature channel; that limit is inherited from ch0 itself. (7) No real data is used. A real-data follow-up on the documented AHU -> VAV connections whose handler supply temperature and box discharge temperature are both recorded is possible (the census says the inlet is never recorded, discharge often is) and needs its own pre-registration; because those documented connections are unverified, it would test consistency with the documentation, not accuracy. (8) Part A's ceiling is model-free and optimistic and ranks signals without pricing them; the census join describes one corpus.

**Implementation to be written and tested BEFORE the run, then frozen (a deviation is logged, never silently fixed):** `dr/signal_value.py` (channel-subset ceilings, hour selection, spot readings, offsets, the frozen rule; re-using `diagnosability.rank1_accuracy`, `level_evidence`, `build_E`, `slot_layout` and `CHANNEL_SIGNALS` unchanged), `scripts/60_signal_value.py`, `tests/test_signal_value.py`; outputs in `results/docmismatch/dr/signal_value/`. No frozen `active/*.py`, `evidence.py`, `data.py` or `diagnosability.py` file is modified. Not used: SEALED data, T1-T6 suites, any real data.

**Clarifications made while implementing (2026-09-25, written after `dr/signal_value.py` and its 22 tests, BEFORE any Part A or Part B statistic was computed on the simulators; none changes a threshold, a variant or a rule).** (1) CEILING is the maximum over channel subsets of the BUILDING-MEAN rank-1 (one best combination per slot, as an engine would use one weighting), not a per-building maximum; the bootstrap recomputes the maximum inside every resample; a building with no scored unit for a subset is dropped from that subset's mean. (2) The V0 comparator, and the test of whether a season is baseline-limited, use the SAME buildings as the variant being judged (V1: the five with a reheat series). (3) "WORKS at an offset" needs at least one verdict season that is not baseline-limited there; an offset at which both are limited cannot vouch for a variant, so s* stops before it (the literal reading would make it vacuously true). (4) Null control G2: the true owners are shuffled among the boxes (each handler keeps its number of boxes), on all windows, at s = 0, with one draw for the spot variants; chance is the mean of 1/|candidates| over the same scored units; "pooled" = mean over buildings. (5) Offsets are drawn per (box, draw) and held constant over windows and slices; each (building, variant, offset) uses `default_rng([18, building index, variant index, offset index])`, buildings in the order `sys03_psz_ac`, `vav_2ahu_2loops`, `vav_3ahu_2loops`, `dev_F`, `dev_G`, `dev_H`, variants in the order V0-V4, S1, S3, S6, S24. (6) The oracle's reheat threshold uses each box's maximum |reheat| over the window; the flow rules' median and 75th percentile are over the box's own 1008 window hours. (7) Slice hour 0 is taken to be midnight, so "daytime" is slice hours 8-17. (8) Verdict seasons: g0 = windows 0-1, g2 = windows 4-5; g1 = windows 2-3 and g3 = windows 6-7 are reported without a verdict. (9) Code-path evidence before the run: 22 tests pass, including that V0 at offset 0 reproduces `build_E` channel 0 for the real `dev_F` simulator to 1e-12 and its cached Addendum-11 `rank1_window["ch0"]` to 1e-9.

**Result of Addendum 18 (2026-09-25; `scripts/60_signal_value.py` run once as pre-registered, 31 s, after a plumbing smoke test on SYNTHETIC toy buildings only that read no simulator statistic; report `results/docmismatch/dr/signal_value/signal_value_report.md`, raw `signal_value.pkl`; 22 tests pass).** *Gates and checks.* G1 passed in both parts (96 cached Addendum-11 values reproduced to 1e-9; V0 reproduces the cached `ch0` for all six buildings). G2 passed for all nine variants (shuffled-truth accuracy 0.376-0.388 against chance 0.378; V1 0.434 against 0.433). C4: winter windows are colder than summer windows in every building. C2: the oracle identity is NOT exact -- the largest |`t_dis` - `t_in`| over "reheat off" hours is 0.30-0.51 K in the five reheat buildings (the 2% duty threshold still admits a little reheat); logged, not corrected, as pre-registered.
*Part A (frozen labels; VALUE = ceiling lost, building-mean window rank-1, [building-cluster CI]).* `ahu_vav` (ceiling 0.969, ch0 alone): box `t_in` and handler `sat` each CRITICAL (0.319 [0.119, 0.499]; ch1+2 leave 0.650); the two flows MINOR (0.000: ch0 alone is the ceiling either way). `loop_branch` (0.819, all three): both flows MODERATE (0.125 [0.038, 0.254]), `t_in` / `t_sup` MINOR (0.013). `branch_coil` (0.933): branch flow and coil `rate` CRITICAL (no channel left), `t_in` / `t_out` MODERATE (0.050 [0.026, 0.076], on the MODERATE/MINOR boundary). `coil_ahu` (0.850): all four signals CRITICAL (all three channels read all four: handler flow, `sat`, `mat`, coil rate). Census join (descriptive): the critical signals are the ones the census found unrecorded -- box `t_in` 0 buildings, coil rate 0, handler `mat` 0 recorded (30 documented), handler flow 3, the water-side flows and temperatures no node at all -- with the single exception of handler `sat` (30 buildings), which `ahu_vav` needs together with the unrecorded `t_in`.
*Part B (frozen verdicts; six buildings, V1 five; window rank-1 at offset 0, all windows; V0 baseline 0.969, chance 0.378):* **V1 oracle 0.950 FRAGILE (s* = 0); V2 flow-top-50% 0.681 FAILS; V3 flow-top-25% 0.741 FAILS; V4 unfiltered discharge 0.683 FAILS; S1 one reading 0.671 FAILS; S3 three readings 0.766 FAILS; S6 six readings 0.819 FAILS; S24 day logger 0.892 FRAGILE (s* = 0).** The smallest number of readings that works at offset 0 is 24 (the logger); at every offset above 0 none works. S6 clears the 0.80 bar in both verdict seasons (0.805 winter, 0.814 summer) and fails on the within-10-points-of-V0 rule (V0 0.972 / 0.944).
*What the verdict table hides.* (1) **The 0.5 K bar for USABLE could not be met by anything, the baseline included -- a flaw in my pre-registration that I could not see because V0's own offset curve had not been computed.** V0 falls from 0.972 (winter) / 0.944 (summer) at 0 K to 0.819 / 0.818 at 0.25 K, 0.686 / 0.703 at 0.5 K, 0.591 / 0.618 at 1 K and 0.494 / 0.508 at 2 K, so both verdict seasons are baseline-limited (< 0.80) from 0.5 K up and nothing can "work" there; the frozen verdicts stand as written, and the informative reading is relative to V0 (descriptive, no verdict). At 0.25 K, winter / summer: V0 0.819 / 0.818, V1 0.772 / 0.786, S24 0.789 / 0.801, S6 0.742 / 0.752, S3 0.723 / 0.728, V3 0.671 / 0.753, V2 0.615 / 0.689, V4 0.625 / 0.680, S1 0.639 / 0.652. (2) **Pooling hides opposite building behaviour.** At 0 K the unfiltered discharge proxy is exact where there is no reheat (`sys03_psz_ac`: V2 and V4 1.000, chance 0.10) and at chance where the boxes reheat most of the time (`vav_2ahu_2loops`: V4 0.500, chance 0.50; `vav_3ahu_2loops`: V4 0.400, chance 0.333). Six-reading spot accuracy by building ranges from 0.68 (`dev_F`) to 1.000 (`vav_2ahu_2loops`); the CIs are wide throughout (six buildings).
*Exploratory observations -- NOT pre-registered, made to understand two features of the tables on the same design simulators; each needs its own pre-registered follow-up before anything is adopted:* (a) *Why V0 collapses with offset.* The mean |handler supply temperature difference| between a box's true handler and its other candidates (noise-free; handler supply taken as the noise-free inlet of a box on that handler) has median 1.61 K (`sys03_psz_ac`), 1.10 (`vav_2ahu_2loops`), 0.60 (`vav_3ahu_2loops`), 0.22 (`dev_F`), 1.48 with a 10th percentile of 0.14 (`dev_G`), 0.27 K (`dev_H`); in `dev_F` and `dev_H` every wrong candidate is within 0.5 K of the truth on average, so a constant per-box error of that size removes the signal (`dev_F` V0 0.958 -> 0.497 at 0.5 K). How separable real handlers are is exactly what a real building's supply-temperature setpoints decide, and many run the same setpoint: untested here (limitation 6). (b) *Why the flow rules fail.* Boxes spend 63-75% of hours within 5% of their own 5th-percentile flow (a long minimum-flow plateau); reheat is on 78% / 76% / 29% / 17% / 56% of hours (`vav_2ahu_2loops`, `vav_3ahu_2loops`, `dev_F`, `dev_G`, `dev_H`) and still on in 74% / 70% / 26% / 12% / 50% of the hours V2 selects and 67% / 58% / 22% / 4% / 38% of V3's, so a percentile of the box's own flow cannot separate cooling hours from the plateau; a rule keyed to flow clearly above its minimum was not tried (it would be post hoc). (c) *Reading count.* At 0 K accuracy rises with the number of readings (1: 0.671, 3: 0.766, 6: 0.819, 24: 0.892, full trend 0.969).
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (i) In simulation, `ahu_vav`'s 0.97 needs the box inlet sensor to agree with the handler sensors to about 0.25 K; at a constant 0.5 K error it is 0.69-0.70 even with the true inlet temperature trended. So the channel that looked "one sensor away" is fragile to exactly the kind of error real sensors and ducts add, and asking a technician for the signal inherits that fragility; the simulation has no duct gain or calibration error, so the 0.97 was an ideal-case number. (ii) No discharge-temperature proxy and no one-to-six spot reading meets the frozen bar; the day logger comes closest (0.892, within 8 points of the trended baseline at 0 K). (iii) **Part C trigger:** the pre-registered trigger (a variant other than V0 and V1 that is USABLE or FRAGILE) is met by S24 alone; the proxies and the short spot readings did not qualify. Addendum 19 has not been written and nothing has been decided about it. (iv) Untested idea, not acted on: an offset-invariant form of ch0 (compare the box's temperature variation with the handler's, so a constant per-box error drops out) could remove the fragility for trended data and a logger, but not for a single spot reading; it would need its own pre-registration.
*Not concluded:* that any proxy fails on real buildings (six simulators, adiabatic ducts, hourly data); that asking a technician for the inlet temperature is not worth it (the price is not measured and Part C was not run); that 0.25 K is the real offset scale; that Part A's ranking is what the engine would show (model-free upper bound, equal-weight channels).

## Addendum 19 (dated 2026-09-25, written BEFORE any comparison statistic of the troubleshooting working model has been computed): where does the technician-guided tool beat trial-and-error stand-ins, and where does it not -- including when its own assumptions are wrong? (simulation only)

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** The trend-based evidence engine cannot run in most buildings (about 75% of US commercial buildings predate 2000 and only a small share has a building automation system; Addenda 17-18 and the Mortar census), so the project was reframed around a technician-guided troubleshooting aid, `docmismatch.troubleshoot` (working model v0, `TROUBLESHOOT.md`): given the drawings' claim and an equipment list, it suggests one physical check at a time, takes yes / no / unsure / skip, updates its belief, and stops when one cause reaches 0.95. **Every number in it except the `override_room` hit rates (measured in simulation, Addendum 16) is a placeholder** (how often the documentation is wrong, how local errors are, how reliable and how long each check is). A single "saves X%" figure would only echo those placeholders, so this study does not produce one. It produces a MAP: over a grid of truths about the world, with the tool's assumptions held at the placeholders, where does the tool beat stand-ins for trial and error, where does it not, and how much of its advantage survives its assumptions being wrong? The technician interviews are meant to place the real world on that map; nothing here is evidence about real technicians.

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) The v0 code, its 26 unit tests (checked against four deliberately broken versions), and one seeded demonstration transcript (made-up building, Room 204 truly fed by box 2-05: six checks, about 66 minutes, 7 entries): a single run, not a statistic. (2) A timing-only probe of 300 episodes (about 1 ms per episode for `entropy` and `split_half` at 6 candidates, negligible for the habit stand-in), used only to choose the episode count below. (3) Two results from the literature notes in `research_notes/` that shaped the predictions P1-P5: greedy expected-information-gain selection can lose to a prior-independent split-in-half rule when the prior is wrong, and a saving can only exist on the share of call-outs where the layout is the problem. (4) No policy comparison, no grid cell and no classification below has been computed.

**Policies (frozen).** All run against a simulated technician, one episode per (cell, sampled truth). *Tool policies* (`Session` with the tool's ASSUMED default `Settings`, tau 0.95, at most 12 checks): `entropy` (the tool's default), `split_half` (the prior-robust alternative), `random` (any informative check; a reference). *Stand-ins for trial and error* (both ASSUMED constructions of mine, not observed behaviour, like `traversal` in hvac_benchmark): `habit_A` = `sim.simulate_habit` (confirm the documented box moves, then the cheapest act-and-watch check on the documented box and each alternative in candidate order, first yes concludes, all no means "unlisted"); `habit_B` = trace first: a duct trace on the documented box, then on each alternative in candidate order, first yes concludes; if the documented box's trace says yes, confirm it moves (no: acts-fail; yes: cause is elsewhere); all no means "unlisted". Two habits, one fast and cheap and one slow and reliable, so that "beats the habit" does not rest on a single strawman.

**The world (truth), a full-factorial grid of 486 cells (frozen).** Synthetic building, `vav_zone` only: one room documented to box 0; K boxes in total, ceil(K/2) in the documented box's area (including it) and the rest in a second area; the candidate list is all K. Truth per episode is drawn from the TRUE prior, which `make_prior` builds from the true values below.
