(4) *Code-path evidence and disclosures before the run:* 16 new tests for the study pass and the full doc_mismatch suite passes (441 tests); nineteen deliberately broken versions of the study code (off-list truths not mapped to "unlisted", an inverted off-list test, areas not stripped at level min, the truth drawn from the capped list only, a habit sharing the tool's random stream, "actionable" equal to "correct", the grouped-against-min direction reversed, P1 reversed, P2 loosened, P3 lowered, P5 against the wrong habit, a vacuous coverage gate, a completeness gate that ignores stray off-list flags, a box count that includes zones, levels that do not share outcome streams, the tool compared with the habits of the other level, the two worlds' nearness swapped, a G1 that ignores the habits, the cell order changed) were each caught by at least one test, after I added a test for a habit that reads the areas (the first pass missed the G1 mutation). *A harness artefact, disclosed:* two of my mutations swap two names of equal length, so the file size did not change and Python's cached bytecode of the broken version was reused after the source had been restored; this made a test fail against correct source for a while and was traced to the stale bytecode (the source was identical to the original throughout). All three mutation checks (Addenda 21, 22 and this one) were then re-run with bytecode caching disabled and gave 12 of 12, 22 of 22 and 19 of 19 caught, so the counts reported in the earlier clarifications stand. The Addendum 21 and 22 runs were made on code that had passed a fresh full-suite run after their mutation checks. A plumbing smoke run of 3 buildings x 20 episodes wrote to a scratch directory; only headings and row counts were listed and no number in it was read. No comparison of levels, worlds or policies on the full 100 cells has been read.

**Result of Addendum 23 (2026-09-25; `scripts/66_troubleshoot_minimum.py` run once as pre-registered, 98 s on 8 workers; report `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_minimum/minimum_report.md`, per-cell `minimum_cells.csv`, raw `minimum_raw.pkl`).** *Gates:* G1-G6 all passed. *Predictions as operationalised: all five hold* (P1: off-list 0.110 against 0.101 and minutes 38.9 against 38.5 at K8, 0.045 against 0.031 and 38.2 against 37.8 at K40, in the local world; P2: anywhere world, minutes 38.1 against 38.1 and off-list 0.103 against 0.103 at K8, 38.2 against 38.1 and 0.046 against 0.048 at K40; P3: off-list 0.103 at K8 and 0.046 at K40; P4: accuracy 0.959 / 0.909 / 0.961 / 0.907 against `habit_A`'s 0.803 / 0.765 / 0.810 / 0.766; P5: minutes 38.9 / 38.2 / 38.1 / 38.2 against `habit_B`'s 53.9 / 101.7 / 52.4 / 102.2), **but P1 holds only by margins of about 0.4 minutes and one point of coverage**.
*What the numbers say (means over the 25 buildings; level min = equipment list and claims only).* The tool with the minimum data takes 38-39 minutes at every setting; accuracy 96% at K8 and 91% at K40; actionable (right and naming a cause) 86-87%; wrong-and-confident 2%; unresolved 12-13%; the true feeder is off its list in 10-11% of visits at K8 and 5% at K40 (about four in five of the wrong-box visits at K8). **Grouping is worth almost nothing here**: grouped against min changes minutes by -0.4 (local world) or 0.0 to -0.1 (anywhere), the off-list share by -0.009 and -0.015 (local) or 0.000 and +0.002 (anywhere), and 84-100% of buildings are NEITHER (IMPROVES 0-4%, WORSE up to 12% in local / K40). Per building (anywhere, K40, level min), coverage is a matter of building size: 0% off-list in the 17 buildings with at most 39 boxes (K40 covers every box), 1-3% at 43-52 boxes and 6-12% at 87-394 boxes, with the actionable rate falling from 91-93% (13-18 boxes) to 80-87% (87 boxes and up) while minutes stay at 30-45 in every building (capped by K and by the 12-check limit; the perfect-check hand calculation of the disclosure, about 34 checks at 394 boxes, is avoided by giving up, not by finding the box). Against the habits at the same level (context, no verdict): the tool never WINS against both (it is 14-15 minutes slower than `habit_A`, +60% at K8 and +19% at K40); against `habit_A` it BUYS ACCURACY in 72-100% of buildings (96% against 81% at K8, 91% against 77% at K40; wrong-and-confident 2% against 19-24%); against `habit_B` it WINS (at least 10% faster at the same accuracy) in 76-100% of buildings (28% faster at K8, 62% at K40; `habit_B` 8-11% wrong-and-confident).
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (1) With only an equipment list and the claims the tool behaves almost exactly as it did with the air-handler grouping: the same speed, the same accuracy edge over the fast habit and the same speed edge over the slow one. (2) What the minimum data cannot do is find a wrong feeder outside a list it was not told how to narrow: in large buildings roughly 6-12% of all visits end with 'none of these boxes', which counts as correct in the accuracy figure but is not actionable. (3) Why grouping is worth so little here is my interpretation, not tested: the nearness weights are mild (0.25) and a same-handler set in these buildings is large (up to 86 boxes per handler) compared with a list of 8 or 40, so a same-handler list is hardly more likely to contain the true feeder than an identifier-order list. (4) Limits: the identifier order is arbitrary (real ids often follow location, which would help the min level), a technician who actually narrows the search by looking at the building would cover far more than an arbitrary list (Addendum 20's narrowed lists, in which the true feeder was always present, are that optimistic case), there is no trace-and-read-the-tag check and no use of `add_candidate`, and everything is simulated inside the tool's own model family with placeholder minutes and habits I wrote. *Not concluded:* anything about real technicians, real identifier conventions, or how much a real technician's narrowing is worth.


## Addendum 24 (dated 2026-09-25, written BEFORE any id-proximity statistic has been computed): do the identifiers of real campus buildings carry location information, and how much would a candidate list built from id neighbours recover of the wrong feeders that Addendum 23's minimum-data list missed? (cached Mortar metadata and simulation only)

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** Addendum 23 gave the tool only an equipment list and the drawings' claims, with the candidate list being the first K boxes in identifier order, and the true feeder was off that list in 10-11% of visits at K = 8 and 5% at K = 40. A look at three cached layouts (below) suggested that identifiers embed the room number (box `VAVRM108` serves room `RM108`), so the neighbours of the documented box in identifier order may be physical neighbours, at no cost in data. The user asked to do this check.

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) Addenda 19-23 in full, including Addendum 23's per-building table and its result that grouping by air handler was worth about 0.4 minutes and 1 to 1.5 points of coverage (off-list share 0.110 against 0.101 at K8 and 0.045 against 0.031 at K40 in the local world). (2) A feasibility look, before this pre-registration, at the first six (box, zone) pairs of `bldg8`, `bldg2` and `bldg13`: box ids are `VAV` + the zone id (`VAVRM108` / `RM108`; `VAVRM01_1` / `RM01_1`; `bldg13` also has `_MIX` variants); no statistic was computed. P1 below was written with this in mind. (3) Nothing else about the identifiers of these buildings has been examined.

**Definitions (frozen).** *Natural order* of the boxes of a building: identifiers split into digit and non-digit runs, digit runs compared as integers, non-digit runs compared case-insensitively, ties by the plain identifier; the *rank* of a box is its position in that order. *id-near list* for a documented box `d` and cap K: `d`, then the K - 1 other boxes with the smallest absolute rank difference from `d` (ties: the lower rank first), all with equal weight; its length is min(K, boxes), like the other lists. Three **levels** are compared, all using only the equipment list and the claims except the last: **min** (Addendum 23: `d`, then the first K - 1 other boxes in the plain sorted order of the equipment list, equal weights), **idnear** (the id-near list), **grouped** (Addendum 20's list: same air handler first, weights 1 and 0.25; it uses the box -> air handler claims, i.e. one more piece of documentation). Caps K8 and K40; buildings: the 25 of Addendum 20 (Brick metadata only).

**Part A: what the identifiers carry (descriptive, real layouts, no simulation).** A1 *name consistency*: for each documented (box, zone) claim, whether the zone id (upper-cased) is a substring of the box id (upper-cased); reported per building and pooled. A2 *handler lift*: in each building with at least 2 documented handlers, for each box `d` that has a documented handler, among the boxes that have one: `s_id` = the share of `d`'s K - 1 id-nearest boxes (K = 8) that hang on the same handler as `d`; `s_first` = the same for the first K - 1 boxes of the plain sorted order; `base` = (n_h - 1) / (n_handled - 1) for `d`'s handler h (what a random list would give); reported as means over the boxes of a building and as the lift `s_id / base`. Buildings whose lists cover all other boxes (at most K boxes with a handler) have lift 1 by construction and are reported but counted as not favouring either list.

**Part B: coverage of wrong feeders and the tool's outcomes.** *Worlds* (where a wrong feeder lies; the rest as Addendum 23: P(documented feeder wrong) 0.15, P(documented box does not act) 0.15, unlisted share 0.10, assumed checks): **local** (same air handler weight 1, another 0.25) and **anywhere** (uniform), both as in Addendum 23; **idlocal2** and **idlocal8**, ASSUMED adjacency worlds in which the weight of box j as the true feeder of a room documented to box `d` is exp(-|rank_j - rank_d| / lambda) with lambda = 2 and 8 (a wrong feeder is likely a neighbour in id order, as with swapped adjacent rooms). **The idlocal worlds favour the idnear list by construction: they bound what id neighbours could buy if errors were that local; they do not show that errors are.** *Exact coverage*: for a room, the share of the wrong-feeder weight (over all other boxes of the building, in the world's weights) that lies on the list; the *expected off-list share* of visits is 0.135 x (1 - coverage) (0.135 = 0.15 x 0.90); averaged over the rooms (each room equally likely) and then over the buildings. *Simulation*: cells (world, cap, building), 4 x 2 x 25 = 200, indexed lexicographically (world outermost: local, anywhere, idlocal2, idlocal8; then cap; building fastest); 300 episodes per cell, seed `[23, cell]` for the (room, cause) draws (the same constant as Addendum 23, so the first 100 cells draw the same episodes as its cells), the true cause drawn over all boxes of the building and mapped to "unlisted" when it is a wrong feeder off the policy's list, as in Addendum 23; policies `entropy` at the three levels sharing the outcome stream `[23, cell, 0, episode]`; TRUE accounting, 12-check limit and the metrics of Addendum 23 (minutes, accuracy, actionable, wrong-and-confident, unresolved, off-list share); bootstrap `[23, cell, 99]`, 1000 x 300. Comparisons of `idnear` and of `grouped` against `min` on the same episodes use Addendum 21's labels (5% minutes, 0.02 accuracy, paired intervals; IMPROVES / WORSE / TRADE / NEITHER). No verdict rule; descriptive.

**Predictions written before the run (each strict; reported as holds / does not hold).** P1 (the claims follow the names): pooled over the 25 buildings, at least 90% of documented claims have the zone id as a substring of the box id. P2 (ids track the air handlers): in buildings with at least 2 handlers and more than 8 boxes with a handler, `s_id` exceeds `s_first` in at least 70% of them, and the mean lift `s_id / base` over them is at least 1.5. P3 (handler-local world): the mean exact coverage is strictly higher for idnear than for min at both caps, and at least as high for grouped as for idnear. P4 (a ceiling under handler locality): in the local world the expected off-list shares of min and grouped differ by at most 0.015 at each cap (location by air handler cannot buy much in these buildings). P5 (adjacency world, by construction): at lambda = 2 and K8 the expected off-list share of idnear is at most 0.4 times that of min. P6 (tool outcome): in each of the six (world, cap) groups of local, idlocal2 and idlocal8 the mean actionable rate of `entropy@idnear` is strictly above that of `entropy@min`.

**Gates (all evaluated before any reading; a failed gate means NO RESULT).** G1 code path: for the first 100 cells the `min` and `grouped` levels reproduce Addendum 23's stored `entropy@min` and `entropy@grouped` episodes exactly (minutes, checks, guesses, truths, off-list flags); every idnear list starts with the documented box, has no duplicates, has length min(K, boxes) and equals a brute-force nearest-by-rank list for every room of the first building; and the runners reproduce `sim.simulate` (Addendum 20's G1). G2 accounting: Addendum 20's G2. G3 coverage bookkeeping: off-list flags equal a direct count recomputed from the seeds for every policy of every cell; the off-list share is exactly 0 wherever the building has at most K boxes; and the exact expected off-list shares of min and grouped in the local and anywhere worlds agree with Addendum 23's simulated group means within 0.01. G4 reproducibility: the first cell re-run with 50 episodes reproduces the first 50 stored episodes of all three policies. G5 completeness and pairing: all 200 cells x 3 policies present and finite, one set of draws per cell. G6 layout fidelity: as Addendum 20.

**Not used:** timeseries, SEALED data, T1-T6 suites, or any fitted quantity; no download, no external data. Mortar licence as in Addendum 20: only aggregates are written to `results/`.

**Limitations named up front.** (1) If P1 holds, the claims in these buildings are name-derived: the documented box and its room share a name by construction, so they say nothing about whether the drawings are right, and "the documented box's id contains the room" is trivially satisfied; only the NEIGHBOUR structure of ids is tested here. (2) Whether id neighbours are physical neighbours is not tested (there is no location data in Brick); Part A tests only agreement with the air-handler grouping, a coarser structure. (3) Handler-local truth is the assumption of Addenda 19-23; the adjacency worlds are a further assumption and favour idnear by construction. Real documentation errors may be neither. (4) Mortar is a research corpus of campus buildings with Brick models; identifier conventions in hospitals, corporate buildings and other campuses may differ. (5) Errors and answers come from the tool's own model family; minutes and hit rates are placeholders; `vav_zone` only; the tool only (no habits). (6) A wrong feeder is still found by checking candidates one at a time; the tag-read check is not modelled.

**Implementation to be written and tested BEFORE the run, then frozen (a deviation is logged, never silently fixed):** `troubleshoot/idproximity.py` (natural order, id-near lists, Part A metrics, exact coverage, the adjacency worlds, the runner, analysis, predictions, gates), `scripts/67_troubleshoot_idproximity.py`, `tests/test_troubleshoot_idproximity.py`; outputs in `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_idproximity/`. No existing module is modified. Expected run time a few minutes on 8 workers.

**Clarifications made while implementing (2026-09-25, written after `troubleshoot/idproximity.py`, `scripts/67_troubleshoot_idproximity.py` and `tests/test_troubleshoot_idproximity.py`, BEFORE the 200-cell run was started; none changes a definition, a world, a level, a cap, a label, a prediction or a gate).** (1) The predictions are operationalised as: P1 the sum of consistent claims over the sum of claims, pooled over the 25 buildings, is at least 0.90; P2 among the buildings with at least 2 documented handlers and more than 8 boxes with a handler, the share in which `s_id` exceeds `s_first` is at least 0.70 and the mean of the per-building lifts is at least 1.5 (a building's `s_id`, `s_first` and `base` are means over its boxes that have a handler; its lift is mean `s_id` over mean `base`); P3 in the local world, for each cap, the mean over the 25 buildings of the exact coverage is strictly higher for idnear than for min and not lower for grouped than for idnear; P4 in the local world, for each cap, 0.135 x (1 - mean coverage) of min and of grouped differ by at most 0.015; P5 the same quantity of idnear at lambda = 2 and K8 is at most 0.4 times that of min; P6 in each of the six (world, cap) groups of local, idlocal2 and idlocal8 the mean actionable rate of `entropy@idnear` is strictly above that of `entropy@min`. (2) Natural order and ranks are taken over the boxes (VAV) only. (3) Rooms are equally likely in both the exact coverage and the episodes, so the two can be compared. (4) *Code-path evidence and disclosures before the run:* 25 new tests pass and the full doc_mismatch suite passes (466 tests); twenty-six deliberately broken versions of the study code (digit runs compared as text, a wrong tie-break, a list one box too long, a wrong exponent in the adjacency worlds, a wrong coverage denominator, expected off-list share computed from coverage instead of its complement, a case-sensitive name test, a wrong base rate, a lift for a single handler, a changed seed, levels on different outcome streams, off-list truths not mapped to "unlisted", each of P1-P6 loosened or reversed, full-list buildings counted in P2, a reproduction gate that passes with nothing checked, a loosened exact-against-simulated tolerance, a completeness gate that ignores stray off-list flags, a first-K list in id order, a changed cell order) were run with bytecode caching disabled: 25 were caught by at least one test, and the 26th ("idnear built on the grouped case") is an equivalent mutant, since the candidate list and the weights are replaced wholesale and the base case cannot matter. A plumbing smoke run of 3 buildings x 20 episodes wrote to a scratch directory and only headings and row counts were listed; its G1 and G3 gates failed, as they must with 20 episodes and 3 buildings against Addendum 23's 300 episodes and 25 buildings, and are not enforced in a smoke run. As a separate code-path check, three full-size cells (300 episodes) of the `min` and `grouped` levels reproduced Addendum 23's stored episodes exactly (equality only; no statistic was read). No number of Part A, of the coverage tables or of the `idnear` level on the full data has been read.

**Result of Addendum 24 (2026-09-25; `scripts/67_troubleshoot_idproximity.py` run once as pre-registered, 232 s on 8 workers; report `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_idproximity/idproximity_report.md`, per-cell `idproximity_cells.csv`, raw `idproximity_raw.pkl`).** *Gates:* G1-G6 all passed (the 100 cells shared with Addendum 23 reproduce it exactly; the exact expected off-list shares agree with the simulated ones within 0.0046). *Predictions as operationalised: P1-P5 hold, **P6 does not hold*** (details below).
*Part A: what the identifiers carry.* **Name consistency** 2025 of 2124 documented claims (95.3%); one building, `bldg30`, has 0 of 99 (its ids follow another convention) and the other 24 buildings have 100%: in 24 of 25 buildings the claim of a box for a room is name-derived (the box id contains the room id), so it is not independent documentation of whether the room is really fed by that box. **Handler lift:** over the 17 buildings with at least 2 documented handlers and more than 8 boxes with a handler, the 7 id-nearest boxes hang on the same air handler as the box in 76% of cases on average, against 34% for the first-K list and 38% for a random list (mean lift 2.73; id beats first in 16 of 17 buildings); the lift is largest in buildings with many handlers (`bldg5` 7.00, `bldg32` 5.62, `bldg6` 5.21, `bldg11` 3.96, `bldg12` 3.50, `bldg37` 3.17) and near 1 where there are two (`bldg2` 1.05, `bldg20` 1.16); 8 buildings have a single handler and give no contrast. The first-K list is slightly worse than random, because it covers the start of the building, not the box's neighbourhood.
*Part B: coverage and the tool (means over the 25 buildings; minutes / accuracy / actionable).* **Errors local to the air handler (the assumption of Addenda 19-23):** exact coverage of a wrong feeder min / idnear / grouped is 20 / 24 / 27% at K8 and 67 / 72 / 76% at K40, so an id-neighbour list closes about 60% of the gap between the arbitrary list and full grouping, but the gap is small: idnear against min changes minutes by -0.3 (K8) and 0.0 (K40), the off-list share by -0.004 and -0.010, the actionable rate by +0.004 and +0.001 (100% and 88% of buildings NEITHER; 12% WORSE at K40 on accuracy, -0.007). **Errors anywhere:** all three lists cover the same (20% at K8, 67% at K40) and every difference is within 0.3 minutes and 0.003. **ASSUMED adjacent-room errors (lambda 2, favouring idnear by construction):** at K8 the id list covers 86% against 21% (min) and 30% (grouped); off-list 0.107 -> 0.019; the tool takes 34 against 38 minutes, its actionable rate is 94% against 87%, unresolved 5% against 12%, IMPROVES in 100% of the buildings (-3.5 minutes, +6.9 points actionable). At lambda 8 and K8: coverage 47% against 20%, actionable +3.3 points, IMPROVES in 36%. **At K40 the coverage gain does not turn into answers:** off-list falls to 0% (lambda 2) or 0.4% (lambda 8), yet the actionable rate is unchanged (0.868 against 0.869; 0.873 against 0.871), minutes are unchanged, and accuracy FALLS by 3.3 and 2.8 points (WORSE in 52% and 48% of buildings); interpretation, not tested: with equal weights on 40 candidates the tool cannot find the feeder within its 12 checks even when it is on the list, and `min` is credited with a correct "unlisted" for the off-list visits it cannot resolve. `grouped` behaves as in Addendum 23 (about 0.4 minutes and 1 to 1.5 points of coverage in the local world).
*Predictions.* P1 0.953 (holds); P2 id beats first in 0.94 of 17 buildings, mean lift 2.73 (holds); P3 local coverage 0.200 / 0.242 / 0.268 at K8 and 0.667 / 0.723 / 0.762 at K40 (holds); P4 expected off-list 0.108 against 0.099 (K8) and 0.045 against 0.032 (K40), within 0.015 (holds); P5 0.019 against 0.107 (holds, by construction); **P6 does not hold**: idnear's actionable rate is above min's at local/K8 (0.866 against 0.863), idlocal2/K8 (0.936 against 0.867) and idlocal8/K8 (0.897 against 0.864) and idlocal8/K40 (0.873 against 0.871), but ties at local/K40 (0.862) and is below at idlocal2/K40 (0.868 against 0.869).
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (1) The identifiers do carry structure: neighbours in id order mostly hang on the same air handler, strongly so in buildings with many handlers. (2) Under handler-local errors, which is all the earlier studies assumed, an id-neighbour list is worth a fraction of an already small gain (about 0.4 points of named answers at K8). (3) Under adjacent-room errors, which are an assumption and favour the list by construction, it would recover most of what the arbitrary list misses when the list is short (K8), and nothing in named answers when the list is long (K40) unless the tool also weights candidates by id distance, which it does not do here (equal weights, as pre-registered). (4) Whether real documentation errors are adjacent-room errors cannot be learned from these files; that needs real mismatches (work orders with resolution notes, or technicians). (5) 24 of 25 layouts carry claims that are name-derived, which limits what these buildings can say about how often real drawings are wrong; `bldg30` shows that conventions differ. (6) Mortar is a research corpus of campus buildings; other conventions will differ. *Not concluded:* that real errors are id-local; anything about hospitals or corporate buildings; anything about real technicians.


## Addendum 25 (dated 2026-09-25, written BEFORE the id-distance weighting has been run in any study): does weighting the candidates by their distance in id order make the tool find wrong feeders that an equal-weight id-neighbour list cannot, and is it harmless where errors are not id-local? (cached Mortar metadata and simulation only)

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** Addendum 24 found that a candidate list built from id neighbours covers wrong feeders better than an arbitrary list, but that with 40 candidates the tool's named-answer rate did not improve even when the true feeder was on the list (it weights the 40 equally and stops after 12 checks). The user asked to make the tool weight candidates by id distance. This addendum builds that as an option of the tool and tests it.

**What is built (before the run; additive, defaults unchanged).** `model.Settings` gains `nearness` (`"area"`, the default and the existing behaviour, or `"id"`) and `id_decay` (default 4.0, a placeholder fixed a priori as the geometric middle of the adjacency worlds' 2 and 8; it is not tuned). With `nearness = "id"`, `building.candidates_for` returns the documented owner followed by the K - 1 other owners nearest to it in natural id order (digit runs compared as integers; ties: the lower rank first), with the weight of a non-documented candidate j equal to exp(-|rank_j - rank_doc| / `id_decay`) (weight 1 for the documented one); `Session.add_candidate` applies the same rule to a piece of equipment the technician adds; the CLI gets `--nearness {area,id}` and `--id-decay`. Ranks are taken over the equipment of the owner kind. Nothing else in the tool changes, and with `nearness = "area"` every existing behaviour is identical (tested).

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) Addenda 19-24 in full, in particular Addendum 24's per-world results: with equal weights the id-near list left the named-answer rate unchanged at K40 in every world (0.868 against 0.869 at lambda 2) although the true feeder was always on the list, and its accuracy fell by about 3 points; (2) the reasoning that weights concentrated near the documented box should let the tool test the nearest candidates first and so find an id-near feeder within its 12 checks; the predictions below were written with that in mind. (3) No run of any weighting other than 1 and the handler weights of Addendum 20 has been made.

**Levels (all use only the equipment list and the claims, except `grouped`).** `min` (Addendum 23: the first K boxes of the equipment list, equal weights), `idnear` (Addendum 24: the id-near list, equal weights), `idw2`, `idw4`, `idw8` (the same id-near list with the tool's `id_decay` = 2, 4, 8; produced by the tool's new `nearness = "id"` option), `grouped` (Addendum 20: same air handler first, weights 1 and 0.25). The primary tool variant is **`idw4`**; `idw2` and `idw8` are reported for robustness to a wrong decay. Caps K8 and K40; the 25 buildings of Addendum 20 (Brick metadata only).

**Worlds (frozen; where a wrong feeder lies; the rest as Addendum 23: P(documented feeder wrong) 0.15, P(acts-fail) 0.15, unlisted share 0.10, assumed checks).** `local` (same air handler weight 1, another 0.25), `anywhere` (uniform), `idlocal2` and `idlocal8` (Addendum 24's ASSUMED adjacency worlds: weight exp(-|rank_j - rank_d| / lambda)) and `idlocal4` (the same with lambda = 4, ASSUMED, the world that matches `idw4`). **The adjacency worlds favour id weighting by construction: they bound what it could buy if errors were that local; they do not show that errors are.**

**Cells, episodes, pairing (frozen).** A cell is (world, cap, building): 5 x 2 x 25 = 250, indexed lexicographically with the worlds in the order local, anywhere, idlocal2, idlocal8, idlocal4 (so the first 200 cells are Addendum 24's cells), cap next, building fastest. 300 episodes per cell, seed `[23, cell]` for the (room, cause) draws (the true cause is drawn over all boxes of the building and mapped to "unlisted" when it is a wrong feeder off the policy's list, as in Addenda 23-24); six policies, `entropy` at the six levels, sharing the outcome stream `[23, cell, 0, episode]`; TRUE accounting, 12-check limit and the metrics of Addendum 23 (minutes, accuracy, actionable, wrong-and-confident, unresolved, off-list share) plus the mean number of checks; bootstrap `[23, cell, 99]`, 1000 x 300. The `idw` levels are compared with `idnear` (the same list with equal weights: it isolates the weighting) and with `min`, `idnear` and `grouped` with `min`, using Addendum 21's labels (5% minutes, 0.02 accuracy, paired intervals; IMPROVES / WORSE / TRADE / NEITHER).

**Predictions written before the run (each strict; reported as holds / does not hold; means over the 25 buildings of a group).** P1 (weighting fixes the long list): at K40 in each of `idlocal2`, `idlocal4` and `idlocal8`, the actionable rate of `idw4` is above that of `idnear`. P2 (and it beats the arbitrary list): at K40 in `idlocal2` and `idlocal4`, the actionable rate of `idw4` is at least 0.04 above that of `min`. P3 (harmless when errors are anywhere): in the `anywhere` world at both caps, the actionable rate of `idw4` is within 0.01 of `min`'s and its wrong-and-confident rate is at most 0.01 above `min`'s. P4 (harmless when errors are handler-local): in the `local` world at both caps, the actionable rate of `idw4` is at least that of `idnear` minus 0.005. P5 (robust to a wrong decay): at K40 in each of `idlocal2`, `idlocal4` and `idlocal8`, both `idw2` and `idw8` have an actionable rate above `idnear`'s. P6 (safe): in each of the 10 (world, cap) groups, the wrong-and-confident rate of `idw4` is at most 0.01 above that of `idnear`.
**Decision (about keeping the option; never about shipping it).** **WORTH KEEPING AS AN OPTION** if P1, P3 and P6 all hold (it helps where errors are id-local and lists are long, does no harm where they are not, and is safe); otherwise **NOT WORTH KEEPING**. P2, P4 and P5 are reported and carry no verdict.

**Gates (all evaluated before any reading; a failed gate means NO RESULT).** G1 code path: for the first 200 cells the `min`, `idnear` and `grouped` levels reproduce Addendum 24's stored episodes exactly; for every room of the first building and both caps the candidates of the tool's id-mode case equal Addendum 24's `id_near_list` and the non-documented weights equal exp(-|rank difference| / lambda) for lambda in {2, 4, 8}; the default `Settings` has `nearness = "area"` and cases built with and without `nearness = "area"` are identical; the runners reproduce `sim.simulate` (Addendum 20's G1). G2 accounting: Addendum 20's G2. G3 coverage bookkeeping: off-list flags equal a direct count recomputed from the seeds for every policy of every cell, and the off-list share is exactly 0 wherever the building has at most K boxes; and the drawn wrong feeders are ordered by rank distance as they should be: the mean absolute rank distance over all drawn wrong feeders is strictly increasing over `idlocal2`, `idlocal4`, `idlocal8`, `anywhere`. G4 reproducibility: the first cell re-run with 50 episodes reproduces the first 50 stored episodes of all six policies. G5 completeness and pairing: all 250 cells x 6 policies present and finite, one set of draws per cell. G6 layout fidelity: as Addendum 20.

**Not used:** timeseries, SEALED data, T1-T6 suites, or any fitted quantity; no download, no external data. Mortar licence as in Addendum 20: only aggregates are written to `results/`.

**Limitations named up front.** (1) The tool's decay is a placeholder, and the adjacency worlds are assumptions that favour the weighting by construction; whether real documentation errors are adjacent-room errors is unknown, and no file at hand can tell. (2) Id order stands for physical adjacency only where ids follow rooms; in 24 of the 25 buildings the ids embed the room (Addendum 24), one building (`bldg30`) follows another convention, and other buildings will differ. Weighting by id distance where ids do not follow location would mislead the tool; the `anywhere` world is the only test of that here. (3) Errors and answers come from the tool's own model family; minutes and hit rates are placeholders; `vav_zone` only; the tool only (no habits). (4) A wrong feeder is still found by checking candidates one at a time; the tag-read check is not modelled.

**Implementation to be written and tested BEFORE the run, then frozen (a deviation is logged, never silently fixed):** the feature in `troubleshoot/model.py`, `building.py`, `session.py` and `cli.py` (additive), `troubleshoot/study_idweight.py`, `scripts/68_troubleshoot_idweight.py`, `tests/test_troubleshoot_idweight.py`; outputs in `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_idweight/`. Expected run time about 10 minutes on 8 workers.

**Clarifications made while implementing (2026-09-25, written after the feature in `troubleshoot/model.py`, `building.py`, `session.py`, `cli.py`, and after `troubleshoot/study_idweight.py`, `scripts/68_troubleshoot_idweight.py` and `tests/test_troubleshoot_idweight.py`, BEFORE the 250-cell run was started; none changes a level, a world, a cap, the tool's decay, a label, a prediction, the decision rule or a gate).** (1) The predictions are operationalised, with every quantity a mean over the 25 buildings of a (world, cap) group, as: P1 at K40, in each of idlocal2, idlocal4 and idlocal8, actionable(idw4) > actionable(idnear); P2 at K40 in idlocal2 and idlocal4, actionable(idw4) >= actionable(min) + 0.04; P3 in the anywhere world at both caps, |actionable(idw4) - actionable(min)| <= 0.01 and wrong-and-confident(idw4) - wrong-and-confident(min) <= 0.01; P4 in the local world at both caps, actionable(idw4) >= actionable(idnear) - 0.005; P5 at K40 in each adjacency world, actionable(idw2) > actionable(idnear) and actionable(idw8) > actionable(idnear); P6 in each of the 10 (world, cap) groups, wrong-and-confident(idw4) - wrong-and-confident(idnear) <= 0.01. (2) The tool's ranks are taken over the equipment of the owner kind (boxes for `vav_zone`, air handlers for `ahu_vav`). (3) *Code-path evidence and disclosures before the run:* the 166 troubleshooting tests that existed before the feature pass unchanged (the defaults are unchanged), 23 new tests for the feature and the study pass, and the full doc_mismatch suite passes (489 tests); thirty deliberately broken versions of the feature and of the study code (digit runs compared as text, ranks sorted by plain id, a wrong tie-break, a wrong exponent, flat weights in id mode, the documented box not first, the default nearness changed, the nearness and the decay not validated, an added candidate that ignores id mode, a CLI that drops the decay, the wrong decay for idw4 or idlocal4, idw levels built in area mode, a wrong world exponent, levels on different outcome streams, off-list truths not mapped to "unlisted", each of P1-P6 loosened or reversed, the decision reading P2 instead of P3, a reproduction gate that passes with nothing checked, a removed world-ordering gate, a G1 that does not check the weights, a changed cell order, a comparison against the wrong baseline) were run with bytecode caching disabled and each was caught by at least one test (one mutation's pattern needed correcting and was re-run separately). One test I wrote first assumed that after the documented box was excluded the next recommended check would be on a neighbour; it was wrong (the tool preferred a cheap diffuser check on the documented box) and was replaced by a test on the posterior ranking: a test bug, not a code bug. A plumbing smoke run of 3 buildings x 20 episodes wrote to a scratch directory; only headings, row counts and its gates table were read, and the decision string it contains was not displayed; its G1 and G3 gates failed, as they must with 20 episodes against Addendum 24's 300 and with 20-episode rank distances (idlocal2 and idlocal4 came out in the wrong order by chance); gates are not enforced in a smoke run. Two further sanity checks read no tool outcome: (a) three full-size cells (300 episodes) reproduced Addendum 24's stored episodes exactly for the `min`, `idnear` and `grouped` levels; (b) over the draws of all 250 cells (no tool episodes) the mean rank distance of a drawn wrong feeder is 2.47 (idlocal2), 3.94 (idlocal4), 6.32 (idlocal8), 29.08 (anywhere) and 25.42 (local), the order G3 requires. Nothing about the idw levels' outcomes has been read.

**Result of Addendum 25 (2026-09-25; `scripts/68_troubleshoot_idweight.py` run once as pre-registered, 624 s on 8 workers; report `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_idweight/idweight_report.md`, per-cell `idweight_cells.csv`, raw `idweight_raw.pkl`).** *Gates:* G1-G6 all passed (the 200 cells shared with Addendum 24 reproduce it exactly; the drawn wrong feeders' mean rank distance is 2.5, 3.9, 6.3 and 29.1 in idlocal2, idlocal4, idlocal8 and anywhere). *Frozen decision:* **WORTH KEEPING AS AN OPTION**; **all six predictions hold** (P1: idw4 against idnear at K40 0.937 / 0.868 in idlocal2, 0.919 / 0.867 in idlocal4, 0.902 / 0.873 in idlocal8; P2: idw4 against min 0.937 / 0.869 and 0.919 / 0.866; P3: anywhere world, actionable 0.865 against 0.867 (K8) and 0.861 against 0.862 (K40), wrong-and-confident 0.022 against 0.022 and 0.024 against 0.021; P4: local world 0.865 against 0.866 (K8) and 0.867 against 0.862 (K40); P5: idw2 and idw8 at K40 0.937 / 0.937, 0.918 / 0.919, 0.901 / 0.902 against idnear's 0.868, 0.867, 0.873; P6: the largest wrong-and-confident excess over idnear is +0.0025).
*What the numbers say (means over the 25 buildings; minutes / actionable / unresolved unless stated).* **Where wrong feeders sit near the documented box in id order (ASSUMED worlds, favouring the option by construction):** at K40 the weighting does what the equal-weight list could not: lambda 2: idnear 37 / 87% / 12% against idw4 33 / 94% / 5% (idw4 against idnear: -4.1 minutes, +0.069 actionable, accuracy +0.069, IMPROVES in 100% of buildings); lambda 4: 38 / 87% / 13% against 35 / 92% / 8% (-2.8 minutes, +0.052, 88% IMPROVES); lambda 8: 37 / 87% / 12% against 35 / 90% / 9% (-1.7 minutes, +0.029, 68% IMPROVES). At K8 the list already does the work and the weights add almost nothing (idw4 against idnear +0.003, +0.000 and +0.001 actionable at lambda 2, 4, 8, with -0.6 to +0.6 minutes; idw4 against min +0.072, +0.052 and +0.034 actionable and -4.1, -2.3 and -1.1 minutes). The three decays 2, 4 and 8 give the same result to within 0.001 in actionable at K40 in every adjacency world, so the placeholder decay hardly matters. `grouped` is worse than the id levels there (88% against 94% at lambda 2, K40). **Where errors are local to the air handler (the assumption of Addenda 19-24):** idw4 against min is +0.006 actionable at K40 (-0.6 minutes) and +0.003 at K8 (+0.6 minutes): nothing. **Where errors are anywhere (an id order that says nothing about location):** idw4 against min changes actionable by -0.002 (K8) and -0.000 (K40), wrong-and-confident by +0.000 and +0.003, minutes by +1.0 (K8; 4% of buildings WORSE) and -0.5 (K40): harmless. **Safety:** the wrong-and-confident rate stays at 2% everywhere and the largest excess of idw4 over idnear is +0.0025.
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (1) The option works as designed: weighting candidates by id distance turns a long id-near list into a useful one when wrong feeders are near the documented box, and costs about a minute (about 2%) at K8 and nothing measurable elsewhere when they are not; at K8 the list, not the weights, does most of the work; at K40 the weights do all of it. (2) The size of the gain is set entirely by the assumed world: 3 to 7 points of visits ending with a named cause (and 5 to 11% fewer minutes) if wrong feeders are that close in id order, about half a point if they are merely on the same air handler, none if they are anywhere. (3) Whether real documentation errors are near the documented box in id order is unknown and cannot be learned from these files; that needs real mismatches (work orders with resolution notes, or technicians). (4) The anywhere world stands for ids that carry no location (as in `bldg30`), so the option is also harmless there. (5) The decision is about keeping the option in the tool; nothing has been shipped or made a default. *Not concluded:* that real errors are id-local; anything about hospitals or corporate buildings; anything about real technicians.


## Addendum 26 (dated 2026-09-25, written BEFORE any habit has been run on an id-near candidate list): how does the id-weighted tool compare with the fast trial-and-error stand-in when both get the same information? (cached Mortar metadata and simulation only)

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** After Addendum 25 the user asked how the tool now compares with the "fast technician". Addendum 25 ran only the tool. Addendum 23 ran the two stand-in habits only on the arbitrary first-K list and on the air-handler list, so it says nothing about a fast technician who also works through the id-near list. For a fair comparison the habits must get the same candidate list as the tool. **The "fast technician" is `habit_A`, a stand-in I wrote, not a real technician.**

**Stand-ins (frozen; exactly as in Addendum 19; they read the candidate ORDER and never the weights).** `habit_A` (fast): confirm that the documented box moves (`confirm_acts`), then run the cheapest act-and-watch check (by the assumed minutes) on the documented box and on each alternative in list order; the first yes concludes; all no means "unlisted". `habit_B` (slow, careful): a duct trace on the documented box, then on each alternative in list order; the first yes concludes (a yes on the documented box is followed by a `confirm_acts`); all no means "unlisted". Like the tool they are charged the TRUE minutes and answered from the TRUE hit rates.

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) Addenda 19-25 in full: in particular Addendum 23's result on the arbitrary and air-handler lists (the tool 60% slower than `habit_A` at K8 and 19% at K40, but 96% against 81% accurate and 2% against 19-24% wrong-and-confident; 28% and 62% faster than `habit_B`), and Addendum 25's tool numbers with the id-weighted list (about 33-35 minutes and 90-94% actionable at K40 in the assumed nearby-error worlds). (2) Nothing about how the habits behave on an id-near list has been computed. (3) My expectation, written down here so that it can be wrong: the fast stand-in benefits from a near-first order at least as much as the tool does, so the tool will still not be faster than it.

**Policies and levels (frozen).** Six policies per cell: `entropy@min` (Addendum 23's list, equal weights) and `entropy@idw4` (the id-near list weighted with the tool's `nearness = "id"`, `id_decay` 4: the primary tool variant of Addendum 25), `habit_A@min` and `habit_B@min` (the first-K list), `habit_A@idnear` and `habit_B@idnear` (the id-near list, in order of rank distance, ties the lower rank first). The tool and the habits at the same list share their candidates; the habits at `idnear` see exactly the candidates the tool sees at `idw4`.

**Worlds, cells, episodes (frozen; as Addendum 25).** Worlds `local`, `anywhere`, `idlocal2`, `idlocal8`, `idlocal4` (the last three ASSUMED adjacency worlds that favour id-ordered lists by construction, for the habits as much as for the tool), caps K8 and K40, the 25 buildings of Addendum 20 (Brick metadata only): 250 cells, indexed as in Addendum 25; 300 episodes per cell, seed `[23, cell]` for the (room, cause) draws (the same draws as Addenda 24 and 25, and, for the first 100 cells, as Addendum 23), the true cause drawn over all boxes and mapped to "unlisted" when it is a wrong feeder off the policy's list; the tool's outcome stream `[23, cell, 0, episode]`, `habit_A`'s `[23, cell, 1, episode]`, `habit_B`'s `[23, cell, 2, episode]` (the two levels of a habit share their stream); TRUE accounting; the tool stops after 12 checks, the habits run to exhaustion, as in Addendum 23; metrics as Addendum 25 (minutes, accuracy, actionable, wrong-and-confident, unresolved, mean checks); bootstrap `[23, cell, 99]`, 1000 x 300.

**Comparisons (frozen; descriptive, no verdict rule).** Per cell, with Addendum 19's classification (WINS / LOSES / NEITHER at the 10% and 0.02 margins with the paired interval on minutes, and its trade-off flag) and Addendum 20's BUYS ACCURACY label (the tool at least 10% slower, at least 0.05 more accurate, paired interval above 0): **(a) primary, same information:** `entropy@idw4` against `habit_A@idnear` and against `habit_B@idnear`, and against BOTH (Addendum 19's `combine`); **(b) context:** `entropy@min` against `habit_A@min` and `habit_B@min` (Addendum 23's pairing) and `entropy@idw4` against `habit_A@min` and `habit_B@min` (the tool with id information against a technician without); **(c)** what each habit gains from the id list: the paired difference `habit@idnear` minus `habit@min` in minutes, accuracy and actionable rate. Reported as shares of the 25 buildings per (world, cap) group and as group means.

**Predictions written before the run (each strict; reported as holds / does not hold; means over the 25 buildings of a group; "all groups" = the 10 (world, cap) groups).** P1 (the tool is still not faster than the fast stand-in): in all groups the mean minutes of `habit_A@idnear` are below those of `entropy@idw4`. P2 (but it is more accurate): in all groups the mean accuracy of `entropy@idw4` is above that of `habit_A@idnear`. P3 (and much safer): in all groups the mean wrong-and-confident rate of `entropy@idw4` is at least 0.05 below that of `habit_A@idnear`. P4 (faster than the slow stand-in): in all groups the mean minutes of `entropy@idw4` are below those of `habit_B@idnear`. P5 (the id list helps the fast stand-in too): in the `idlocal2` world at both caps the mean actionable rate of `habit_A@idnear` is at least 0.03 above that of `habit_A@min`.

**Gates (all evaluated before any reading; a failed gate means NO RESULT).** G1 code path: for every cell the `entropy@min` and `entropy@idw4` episodes reproduce Addendum 25's stored episodes exactly, and for the first 100 cells the `habit_A@min` and `habit_B@min` episodes reproduce Addendum 23's stored ones exactly (minutes, checks, guesses, truths, off-list flags); the habits give identical results on the id-near list whatever weights the case holds (compared with the tool's id-weighted case of the same candidates); the runners reproduce `sim.simulate` (Addendum 20's G1). G2 accounting: Addendum 20's G2. G3 coverage bookkeeping: off-list flags equal a direct count recomputed from the seeds for every policy of every cell (each against its own list), and the off-list share is exactly 0 wherever the building has at most K boxes. G4 reproducibility: the first cell re-run with 50 episodes reproduces the first 50 stored episodes of all six policies. G5 completeness and pairing: all 250 cells x 6 policies present and finite, one set of draws per cell. G6 layout fidelity: as Addendum 20.

**Not used:** timeseries, SEALED data, T1-T6 suites, or any fitted quantity; no download, no external data. Mortar licence as in Addendum 20: only aggregates are written to `results/`.

**Limitations named up front.** (1) Both habits are my constructions and far simpler than a real technician: they use no symptoms, no history, no knowledge of the building beyond the order of the list, and stop at the first yes; a real fast technician who knows the building may narrow the search better than id order does, or worse. (2) The adjacency worlds favour id-ordered lists for the habits as much as for the tool; the `local` and `anywhere` worlds do not. (3) Minutes and hit rates are placeholders; the habits and the tool are charged the same. (4) The tool stops after 12 checks and the habits do not (as in Addendum 23), which flatters the tool on minutes in the visits that no list resolves. (5) `vav_zone` only; nothing here is evidence about real technicians.

**Implementation to be written and tested BEFORE the run, then frozen (a deviation is logged, never silently fixed):** `troubleshoot/study_fast.py`, `scripts/69_troubleshoot_fast.py`, `tests/test_troubleshoot_fast.py`; outputs in `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_fast/`. No existing module is modified. Expected run time about 5 minutes on 8 workers.

**Clarifications made while implementing (2026-09-25, written after `troubleshoot/study_fast.py`, `scripts/69_troubleshoot_fast.py` and `tests/test_troubleshoot_fast.py`, BEFORE the 250-cell run was started; none changes a policy, a list, a world, a comparison, a prediction or a gate).** (1) The predictions are operationalised, with every quantity a mean over the 25 buildings of a (world, cap) group and "all groups" the 10 groups of 5 worlds x 2 caps, as: P1 minutes(`habit_A@idnear`) < minutes(`entropy@idw4`) in all groups; P2 accuracy(`entropy@idw4`) > accuracy(`habit_A@idnear`) in all groups; P3 wrong-and-confident(`habit_A@idnear`) - wrong-and-confident(`entropy@idw4`) >= 0.05 in all groups; P4 minutes(`entropy@idw4`) < minutes(`habit_B@idnear`) in all groups; P5 in the idlocal2 world at both caps actionable(`habit_A@idnear`) >= actionable(`habit_A@min`) + 0.03. (2) The policies of a cell share Addendum 25's draws (the same seed and cell index), so `entropy@min` and `entropy@idw4` reproduce Addendum 25 and, in the first 100 cells, `habit_A@min` and `habit_B@min` reproduce Addendum 23. (3) The "against BOTH habits" combination is Addendum 19's `combine` applied to three pairs of policies: the tool with the id list against the two habits on the id list, the tool on the first-K list against the two habits on it, and the tool with the id list against the two habits on the first-K list. (4) *Code-path evidence and disclosures before the run:* 13 new tests pass and the full doc_mismatch suite passes (502 tests); twenty deliberately broken versions of the study code (a changed habit stream, `idw4` built as `idnear`, every habit run as `habit_A`, off-list truths not mapped to "unlisted", the arguments of the classification swapped, the combination looking at one habit only, a reversed gain, each of P1-P5 loosened or pointed at the wrong policy or world, a reproduction gate that ignores the id-weighted tool or `habit_A` or passes with nothing checked, a weights gate that checks one habit, a coverage gate that uses one list for every policy, a completeness gate that ignores stray off-list flags, a combination that counts the same habit twice) were run with bytecode caching disabled and each was caught by at least one test. A plumbing smoke run of 3 buildings x 20 episodes wrote to a scratch directory; only headings, row counts and its gates table were read; its G1 failed, as it must with 20 episodes against Addenda 23 and 25's 300, and gates are not enforced in a smoke run. As a separate code-path check, four full-size cells (300 episodes) reproduced Addendum 25's stored episodes for `entropy@min` and `entropy@idw4` exactly and three of them (those among Addendum 23's cells) reproduced its stored episodes for `habit_A@min` and `habit_B@min` exactly (equality only; no statistic was read). No number about the habits on the id-near list has been read.

**Result of Addendum 26 (2026-09-25; `scripts/69_troubleshoot_fast.py` run once as pre-registered, 357 s on 8 workers; report `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_fast/fast_report.md`, per-cell `fast_cells.csv`, raw `fast_raw.pkl`).** *Gates:* G1-G6 all passed (all 250 cells reproduce Addendum 25 for the tool and the first 100 reproduce Addendum 23 for the habits exactly; the habits give identical results with equal and with id weights). *Predictions as operationalised: all five hold* (P1 smallest margin +5.6 minutes at anywhere / K40: 32.1 for `habit_A@idnear` against 37.7 for the tool; P2 smallest margin +0.119 accuracy at idlocal2 / K40: 0.947 against 0.828; P3 smallest margin +0.147 at idlocal2 / K8: wrong-and-confident 0.022 against 0.169; P4 smallest margin +10.2 minutes at idlocal2 / K8: 33.5 against 43.7; P5 actionable 0.817 against 0.759 at K8 and 0.827 against 0.776 at K40).
*What the numbers say (means over the 25 buildings; minutes / accuracy / actionable; the tool is `entropy@idw4`, the fast stand-in `habit_A@idnear`, the slow one `habit_B@idnear`).* **Against the fast stand-in with the same information:** the tool is slower in every group (+52% to +65% at K8, +19% to +30% at K40; for instance 40 against 24 minutes in the local world at K8, 34 against 22 in the lambda 2 world at K8, 33 against 26 in the lambda 2 world at K40) but more accurate (96-97% against 80-83% at K8, 91-95% against 77-83% at K40) and far less often wrong-and-confident (2% against 17-23%), with a higher actionable rate (87-94% against 76-83%). By Addendum 19's classification the tool WINS in 0% of buildings in all 10 groups and LOSES in 0%; by the BUYS ACCURACY label it buys accuracy in 100% of buildings at K8 and in 68% to 96% at K40 (the rest NEITHER). **Against the slow stand-in:** the tool is faster in every group (-23% to -26% minutes at K8, -41% to -60% at K40) at equal or better accuracy (+0.01 to +0.05), WINS in 100% of buildings at K8 and in 80% to 100% at K40. **Against both at once**, every group is 100% NEITHER (it beats one and not the other on time). **The id list helps the stand-ins too:** in the three adjacency worlds `habit_A` gains 0.7 to 5.1 minutes and 2.3 to 5.8 points of actionable rate from the id-near list, `habit_B` 3.2 to 43.5 minutes and 2.3 to 7.9 points; in the local and anywhere worlds nobody gains anything (all within 0.8 points, at most 4.4 minutes for `habit_B` at K40). **The tool with id information against a fast technician without it:** the closest comparison is the lambda 2 world at K40, where the tool is 9% slower (33 against 31 minutes) and 17 points more accurate (95% against 78%); in the lambda 4 and 8 worlds at K40 it is 12% and 14% slower.
*Reading, with the caveats it needs.* (1) The id weighting made the tool better but did not change how it compares with the fast stand-in, because the fast stand-in gains from the same list: the tool is still slower and much more accurate and safer, as in Addendum 23. (2) The gap in time is smallest where the id information is most useful (long lists with wrong feeders near the documented box), 9% to 30%; it is 52% to 65% with short lists. (3) The stand-in's poor accuracy comes from stopping at the first yes and from false alarms that accumulate over checks (each check has a placeholder false-alarm rate); a real fast technician who double-checks would be slower and more accurate than this stand-in, so the size of the tool's accuracy edge is not a finding about real technicians. (4) The tool stops after 12 checks and the stand-ins run to exhaustion, which flatters the tool on minutes. (5) The adjacency worlds favour id-ordered lists for the stand-ins as much as for the tool. *Not concluded:* that the tool beats, or would beat, a real technician on speed or on accuracy; anything about a technician who knows the building.

## Addendum 27 (dated 2026-09-26, written BEFORE the data-tier study has been run at its frozen size): what does each data tier buy the v1 troubleshooting tool, and how much of it survives checks that are less reliable than the tool assumes? (simulation only; NOT YET RUN: waiting for the user's approval)

**Motivation (discussed with the user before writing this).** The user asked for a "v1" of the technician-guided engine, `docmismatch.troubleshoot.v1`, built beside v0: a cause tree for a VAV-zone "room is not getting the right airflow or temperature" complaint refined from two guides (the PNNL VAV O&M page and the ControlsHub VAV airflow-problems guide), a check catalogue in which each check declares what it tests, a per-cause response model, its minutes, its data requirements and a fallback cost when data is missing, and a source label; explicit data tiers T0-T4; technician-declared context; normal / abnormal / unsure observations and numeric readings; and a session log of discrepancies. Three findings of the earlier addenda shaped it: real technicians use many more checks than v0's four; data are scarce, so no check may require data unless it truly cannot be offered without it; and the tool's confident answers are most sensitive to how reliable the checks really are (v0: wrong-and-confident 2.4% with checks as reliable as assumed against 8.8% with worse ones, Addendum 19; about 2% against 8% in Addendum 22). The user also asked that a first study of **what each data tier buys** (minutes, correct-diagnosis rate, confident-wrong rate, with reliabilities worse than assumed as a stress condition) be pre-registered and its code written, and that I then stop and ask before running it. This addendum is that pre-registration. **It has not been run.**

**What is built (before the run; v0 untouched).** The package `troubleshoot/v1/` (`causes.py`, `catalogue.py`, `data.py`, `engine.py`, `sim1.py`, `tables.py`, `cli.py`, `study_tiers.py`), `scripts/70_troubleshoot_v1.py` (the tool) and `scripts/71_troubleshoot_tiers.py` (this study), the example `examples/troubleshoot_example/building_v1.json`, `TROUBLESHOOT_V1.md`, and the tests. v0's modules were not edited: its demo output and three scripted transcripts, captured before any v1 code existed, are compared byte for byte in the tests; a v0-equivalent tree of v1 (`compat_v0=True`: two lumps and the four v0 checks) reproduces v0's sessions and simulated episodes exactly. **The cause tree** has 14 hardware / configuration causes in 10 branches (box mechanical, power and signal, controls, sensing, zone inputs, upstream, reheat, distribution, capacity, other) plus v0's documentation causes ("the documented box is wrong: which one", "fed by something not on the list"); each belongs to one of v0's two "documented box is right" lumps, and with the defaults the leaves of the lumps add up to v0's masses, so the v1 prior refines v0's placeholders rather than replacing them (tested). **The catalogue** has 30 checks: v0's four (text, minutes and rates from v0's own objects) and 26 new ones (control-system reads, checks at the box, at the air handler, in the room), each with `detects` (P(abnormal) under each cause it sees) and a false-alarm rate for every other cause. **Every response probability and every minute is a PLACEHOLDER** except `override_room`'s rates, which v0 took from a simulation (Addendum 16). What is cited is the procedure (which guide section describes it) and, where a guide gives one, the expected value of a numeric reading (22-26 VAC at the actuator, above 2 V control signal, minimum inlet static by box size, within 10% of airflow); the ControlsHub guide is a vendor blog and its numbers were not independently verified. **The tiers** are T0 (nothing but the declared equipment type), T1 (equipment list and claims: v0's input), T2 (+ attributes / design values), T3 (+ control-system access and a point map), T4 (+ trends: a label only; no trend layer is built). A check declares what it needs and a fallback cost (a control-system read is 2 minutes with a point map, 10 without, unavailable without access); the technician can declare the facts that decide whether the best check can be offered (control-system access, an instrument, an occupant, reheat), asked only when the best check cannot be offered without them, and each declaration counts as an entry like a symptom. One v1-only addition that v0 does not have: the tool stops when no remaining check is expected to add at least 0.003 bits per minute (`min_rate`, a PLACEHOLDER; 0 switches it off), because with 30 checks a tail of low-value checks appeared (see the disclosure).

**What has been seen before writing this (disclosure).** (1) Addenda 19-26 in full, and both guides in full (fetched pages, read once each). (2) **Development runs that shaped the catalogue and the defaults; the predictions below are therefore NOT independent of them.** Single scripted sessions ("modal": each check answered with its most likely result) on the made-up six-room example building, for every cause at each of T0-T3, run several times while the catalogue was being built, with their minutes, checks and whether the session concluded read each time. They showed that with control-system reads (T3) sessions were shorter than at T0-T2 for most causes (about a fifth to two thirds of the T0-T2 minutes for controller set-up, capacity, air handler, low static and sensor causes) but not for damper, power and signal faults (about the same or longer), that sessions at T0-T2 often stopped short of 0.95 with the right leading cause, and that a few causes were wrongly led at T0-T2 (a controller fault without control-system access, a capacity fault without design values), and they led to five changes: the walk-to-the-air-handler check was split into a fan check and a duct-pressure gauge check (it could not separate "fan off" from "pressure low"), a controller-lights check was added (a controller fault was unreachable without control-system access), the bits-per-minute stop was added (sessions ran to the 12-check limit on checks adding 0.01-0.04 bits), the undeclared-control-system-access case was changed from "unavailable" to "ask" (found by a unit test), and the caveat about check reliability was added to the tool's output. (3) A 78-episode sample per tier (T1 and T3, the tool's own assumptions, checks answered from the tool's own model, made-up building, hardware causes only) was run once to choose the sample-size bound of a unit test: accuracy among concluded episodes was 100% (34 of 78 concluded at T3, 30 of 78 at T1). It compared no arm with another. (4) A plumbing smoke run of `scripts/71` (2 cells x 20 episodes, written to a scratch directory, not the results directory) was run to test the plumbing; I read its gate table, whose G3 line lists, for each arm, accuracy among concluded episodes (1.000 in every arm) and the number concluded (3 to 11 of about 17 hardware episodes per arm), plus headings and row counts; no comparison table, label or prediction was read. Its G3 failed, as it must at that size (fewer than 30 conclusions). (5) A timing-only probe: about 6 ms per episode. (6) No cell of the 12 has been run at its frozen size.

**The world (frozen).** A synthetic building of 8 rooms, each documented to its own box; boxes 0-3 on air handler 0 (area A), boxes 4-7 on air handler 1 (area B); every box pressure-independent; boxes with reheat are those whose index is not a multiple of 3 (documented as hydronic reheat; the other three have none). The candidate list of a room is all 8 boxes with the tool's nearness weights (1 in the documented box's area, 0.25 outside; the truth uses the same weights, so nearness is correct). Documented attributes (reheat, K-factor, design airflow, inlet diameter; air handler static setpoint) and control-system point names exist for every box and handler (**cover = full**) or only for boxes 0-3 and air handler 0 (**cover = half**); the documented attributes are always right. Each episode draws, from its own stream `[27, cell, 0, episode]` (independent of the arms and of the number of episodes): the room (uniform over the 8), the true cause from the TRUE prior over that room's causes, whether the technician has a gauge or flow hood (probability 0.7) and whether the occupant is there (0.7). **True cause mix (factor `mix`):** *assumed* = the tool's own prior structure (P(documented box wrong) 0.15 of which 10% unlisted, P(acts-fail lump) 0.15, the rest elsewhere, with the catalogue's leaf shares; a reheat fault only on boxes with reheat, its share going to its lump-mates); *shifted* = P(wrong) 0.05, P(acts-fail lump) 0.30 and the leaf shares reversed in rank within each lump (the tool's most common cause becomes the rarest). **True reliability (factor `rel`):** the simulated technician answers each check from the TRUE response probability under the real cause, where TRUE = the tool's catalogue with every probability moved a fraction a toward 0.5 (`catalogue.degrade`): R0 a = 0 (exactly the tool's own model), R1 a = 0.25, R2 a = 0.5 (hit rates fall and false-alarm rates rise together; a = 1 would make every check a coin flip). **The tool's assumptions never change:** default `Settings()`, the catalogue's own numbers and shares, `entropy` selection, tau 0.95, at most 12 checks, `min_rate` 0.003, no symptoms entered.

**The arms (frozen).** T0 (no data: no equipment list, no claims, no attributes, no control-system access), T1, T2, **T2+access** (T2 plus control-system access but no point map, so reads cost 10 minutes) and T3. T4 is T3 (no check uses trends) and is not run. The world's declared facts follow the arm: a box's reheat is known from the file only where an arm has attributes for it, otherwise the tool asks; control-system access is "yes" exactly for the arms that have it (T0-T2 have none, by definition of the tiers); the gauge and the occupant are as drawn, whichever the arm. Arms share the same room, cause, gauge and occupant draws in each cell (paired); the outcome draws of arm a in episode i use `[27, cell, 1 + a, i]` (T0 = 0, T1 = 1, T2 = 2, T2+access = 3, T3 = 4).

**Cells and episodes (frozen).** 12 cells = rel {R0, R1, R2} x mix {assumed, shifted} x cover {full, half}, indexed lexicographically as listed (cover varies fastest; cell 0 = R0 / assumed / full is the **primary cell**); 500 episodes per cell and arm (30,000 arm-episodes); bootstrap 1000 resamples per cell, seed `[27, cell, 99]`. **Comparable episodes:** T0 has no drawings, so it cannot suffer or find a wrong claim; the primary comparison is on the episodes whose true cause is a hardware / configuration cause (about 85% of episodes under mix = assumed, 95% under shifted), which every arm runs. The episodes whose cause is "the documentation is wrong" are run by T1-T3 and reported separately (T0 does not run them).

**Metrics (per arm, cell and subset).** Mean minutes (the tool's own minutes: nothing about the minutes is stressed); accuracy of the final guess (the highest-belief cause when the session ends, exact cause); accuracy of the branch (the guess is in the right branch of the tree); mean checks; mean questions asked; mean entries typed (1 + declared facts + checks); how sessions end (concluded at 0.95 / stopped because nothing left was worth its time / hit the 12-check limit); wrong-and-confident rate (concluded and wrong); accuracy among concluded episodes.

**Frozen comparisons and labels.** For each of six pairs (richer arm minus poorer arm, on the same hardware-cause episodes): T1-T0, T2-T1, T2+access-T2, T3-T2+access (the four adjacent steps), T3-T2 and T3-T0. Addendum 21's frozen labels, unmodified: time state FASTER / SLOWER at a 5% difference in mean minutes with the paired 95% interval clear of 0; accuracy state MORE / LESS at 0.02 with the interval clear of 0; **IMPROVES** = (FASTER and not LESS) or (MORE and not SLOWER); **WORSE** = (SLOWER and not MORE) or (LESS and not FASTER); **TRADE** = (FASTER and LESS) or (SLOWER and MORE); NEITHER otherwise; safety flag REGRESSION if the wrong-and-confident rate is at least 0.02 higher with the interval clear of 0, GAIN if at least 0.02 lower. TRADE is reported in its two kinds (richer arm faster but less accurate; slower but more accurate). (Addendum 20's "BUYS ACCURACY" is not used: under these labels a slower, more accurate arm is already TRADE.) **Frozen reading of an adjacent step, over the 12 cells:** **HELPS** if IMPROVES in at least 75% of the cells and WORSE plus TRADE in at most 10%; **HURTS** if WORSE plus TRADE in more than 25%; MIXED otherwise. The two non-adjacent pairs and the primary-cell deltas are reported without a reading. The 5%, 0.02, 75%, 10% and 25% figures are judgements made before running, not tuned.

**Predictions written before the run (each strict; reported as holds / does not hold; P1-P4 and P9 in the primary cell, on hardware-cause episodes except P9).** P1: mean minutes of T3 are at most 0.75 times those of T2. P2: mean minutes of T3 are at most 0.90 times those of T2+access (the point map matters, not just access). P3: mean minutes of T2 are within 10% of T1's and its accuracy is at least T1's (attributes and design values change little when the checks answer only normal / abnormal). P4: mean minutes of T1 are at least T0's (the documentation hypotheses cost time when the claim is right). P5: for every arm, the wrong-and-confident rate at R2 (mix assumed, cover full) is higher than in the primary cell. P6: the increase of the wrong-and-confident rate from the primary cell to R2 (mix assumed, cover full) is at least as large at T3 as at T1. P7: the absolute difference in mean minutes between T3 and T2+access is smaller at cover = half (R0, assumed) than in the primary cell. P8: for every arm, accuracy at mix = shifted (R0, cover full) is lower than in the primary cell. P9: accuracy of T1 on the documentation-wrong episodes of the primary cell is at least 0.5.

**Gates (all evaluated before any table is read; a failed gate means NO RESULT).** G1 code path: the v0-equivalent tree of v1 reproduces v0's `sim.simulate` exactly (guess, checks, conclusion, entries, minutes) on the study's building for `entropy` and `split_half` (12 episodes each: 2 rooms x 6 truths); and a cell's stored episodes equal direct `simulate_v1` calls on the same draws and seeds. G2 accounting: a scripted transcript at T2+access costs the hand-summed 10 + 10 + 6 = 26 minutes and a control-system read at T3 costs 2; and the first 10 valid episodes of every arm in every R1 and R2 cell, replayed through a fresh session that is given only the recorded answers and declarations, ask and recommend exactly the same things in the same order (the tool's choices do not depend on how reliable the checks really are). G3 honesty: at R0 with mix = assumed, in both coverages, accuracy among concluded hardware-cause episodes is at least 0.90 for every arm, each arm having at least 30 conclusions (a lower value would mean a defect in the belief update; too few conclusions means the gate cannot speak and fails). G4 reproducibility: the first cell re-run with 50 episodes reproduces its first 50 stored episodes of every arm (numbers, guesses and check sequences). G5 completeness and pairing: all 12 cells with all 5 arms present, minutes finite where valid and of full length, every arm valid on every episode except T0, which is valid exactly on the hardware-cause episodes, and every valid episode has a guess. G6 availability: in every stored episode, every check performed was available at its arm given the world's true facts (no control-system read without access, no gauge check without a gauge, no reheat check on a box without reheat, no design-load check without design values, no occupant question without an occupant).

**Not used:** real data of any kind, SEALED data, T1-T6 suites, Mortar, any fitted quantity, any download. Nothing is tuned after this point: the tool's settings, the world, the arms, the labels, the readings and the predictions are frozen here.

**Limitations named up front.** (1) The simulated technician answers each check independently given the cause. Real errors are correlated (a faulty sensor misleads several checks the same way, and a technician who misreads one thing misreads related things), so the tool's real wrong-and-confident rate would be higher than any simulated here. (2) Causes and answers come from the tool's own model family; the catalogue and its numbers are my construction from two guides, one a vendor blog, and every probability and minute is a placeholder, so only orderings and ratios can mean anything. (3) One cause per call-out. A wrong claim AND a fault at the true box is not modelled; after "the documented box is wrong: it is 2-05" a fresh diagnosis of 2-05 would be needed and is not simulated, so accuracy on the documentation-wrong episodes is only about locating the wrong claim. (4) One synthetic building of 8 boxes with nearness known correctly and documented attributes always right; real files have errors and gaps, and the two coverages are the only gap tested. (5) Control-system reads are assumed to be done from a laptop or workstation with no walking time; T0-T2 have no control-system access by definition, whereas a real technician often has it, which is what the T2+access arm approximates. (6) The probabilities that the technician has a gauge (0.7) and that the occupant is there (0.7) are arbitrary. (7) How sessions end depends on the tool's stopping rules (tau 0.95, the 12-check limit and the 0.003 bits-per-minute stop, the last a v1 placeholder): an arm that concludes more often is not thereby more accurate, and accuracy counts the leading cause at the end whether or not it was concluded; read both. (8) The catalogue and the defaults were shaped by seeing single development episodes (disclosure 2), so the predictions are not independent of them and a prediction that holds is weaker evidence than it would be from a fresh design. (9) Placeholder minutes, no parallel waiting (the 30-minute room response is not overlapped), no disruption cost, and T4 is not run. (10) A tier that looks good here says the mechanism can save minutes under the stated assumptions, not that a technician would save any: nothing here is evidence about real technicians, real buildings or real check reliability.

**What would follow.** If T3 against T2+access reads HELPS, the point map (not just access) is what to ask a facilities contact for first; if T2+access against T2 reads HELPS but T3 against T2+access does not, ask for access, not the map. If T2 against T1 reads MIXED or NEITHER, design values matter only through the design-load check, and collecting them is a lower priority. If T1 against T0 costs minutes on hardware-cause episodes but P9 holds, the equipment list and claims are worth loading where the drawings are suspected, and not otherwise. A safety REGRESSION in the R1 / R2 cells for the richer arms would mean that cheap reads make the tool sure faster than they make it right, and would justify a separate pre-registered study of a stricter stopping threshold for those tiers. Whatever the result, the next step toward any real-technician claim is still shadow-mode logging of real call-outs (the interview guide already lists it), because this study cannot move any real-world unknown: the reliability of real checks, the frequency of real causes, and what a real fast technician does.

**Implementation, written and tested BEFORE the run and frozen (a deviation is logged, never silently fixed):** `troubleshoot/v1/study_tiers.py` (grid, world, draws, arms, runner with the tool's own minutes, summaries, labels, readings, predictions, gates), `scripts/71_troubleshoot_tiers.py` (the run, about a minute on 8 workers; raw results pickled first; the report can be regenerated from the pickle), `tests/test_troubleshoot_tiers.py`; outputs in `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_tiers/` (aggregate statistics only). No existing module of v0 or of the other studies is modified.

**Clarifications made while implementing (2026-09-26, written after `troubleshoot/v1/*`, `scripts/70`, `scripts/71`, `TROUBLESHOOT_V1.md` and the tests, BEFORE the study was run at its frozen size; none changes an arm, a cell, the world, a label, a margin, a reading, a prediction or a gate as written above).** (1) The predictions are operationalised exactly as coded in `study_tiers.tier_predictions`: P1 `minutes(T3) <= 0.75 x minutes(T2)`; P2 `minutes(T3) <= 0.90 x minutes(T2+access)`; P3 `|minutes(T2) - minutes(T1)| / minutes(T1) <= 0.10 and accuracy(T2) >= accuracy(T1)`; P4 `minutes(T1) >= minutes(T0)`; P5 `wrong-and-confident(R2) > wrong-and-confident(primary)` for all five arms; P6 `increase(T3) >= increase(T1)` where increase = wrong-and-confident at (R2, assumed, full) minus the primary cell's; P7 `|minutes(T3) - minutes(T2+access)|` at (R0, assumed, half) `<` that in the primary cell; P8 `accuracy(shifted) < accuracy(primary)` for all five arms; P9 `accuracy of T1 on the primary cell's documentation-wrong episodes >= 0.5`. Every quantity is a mean over the episodes named in its sentence above, and "accuracy" is exact-cause accuracy of the final guess whether or not the session concluded. (2) "Stopped" is `Session.status()["stalled"]` at the end (nothing left offered by the rate rule or the information rule); "hit the check limit" is not concluded and not stopped. (3) The bootstrap draws one 1000 x n resample matrix per cell over the hardware-cause episodes, shared by the six comparisons, from `[27, cell, 99]`. (4) T0 keeps no building (tested), so nothing about the equipment can reach it; a T0 session's target is "the box serving the room". (5) The truth is drawn from the same nearness weights the tool uses, and reheat faults are drawn only for boxes that have reheat. (6) The pre-registered runner does not touch the results directory except for the outputs named above. (7) *Two design choices in the study code were changed before this addendum was written and are recorded for transparency:* Addendum 20's "BUYS ACCURACY" label was dropped (under Addendum 21's labels it can never fire, because a slower, more accurate arm is already TRADE; the two kinds of TRADE are reported instead), and gate G2(b) was rewritten from a check that never varied the world into the replay test described above. (8) *What testing found in the code, all fixed before this addendum:* an undeclared control-system access was treated as "no" instead of "ask" (so the tool would never have asked for it at T0-T2), `degrade(check, 0)` was not an exact copy (float rounding would have put the "as assumed" arm 1e-17 away from the tool's own model), `gate_g5` crashed instead of failing on a truncated array, a zero-minute check was not rejected (the rate rule divides by minutes), the command line printed nothing when equipment was added at T0, and the v0-equivalent case rejected an empty declared context.
