The informed arms are deterministic functions of the same visits and answers, so adding them later on the same streams changes no number already printed. If they arrive, they are added in a second run of the same size and reported as such. Every result of the first run carries its n; nothing in it is called confirmed at the registered size.

### Result of the first run of Addendum 43 (2026-10-12; 300 visits per world, NOT the registered 1000; informed tree I2 only, I1 was not ready)

All seven gates passed (G5: the tool and routine equal Addendum 41's stored arrays over the 300 visits run). Output: `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_tree_n300/`. T3-noattr, 18 base cells pooled (5400 visits per arm), mean minutes / best-guess accuracy / declared-and-wrong / not declared: **adaptive tool 63.7 / 69% / 3.0% / 58%**; tree E (confirming) 63.5 / 43% / 22.8% / 37%; E-decisive 58.6 / 43% / 33.4% / 22%; L1 40.2 / 44% / 31.1% / 25%; L2 60.3 / 43% / 21.1% / 32%; **informed tree I2 47.9 / 51% / 10.0% / 62%**; flat checklist 62.6 / 40% / 50.2% / 0%; no checks 0 / 29%. Verdict 1: tool materially better than E in 8 of 18 cells (MIXED); than every blind or informed tree in 0 (ALREADY CLOSE, because the faster trees make it a trade, which the material test does not count). Verdict 2 against E: 14 of 26 stress worlds, E better in 0 (ERODES). Verdict 3 (value at the registered cost box): against E TOOL AHEAD / DEPENDS / BEHIND = 4 / 14 / 0 base cells and 2 / 24 / 0 stress worlds; against I2 0 / 18 / 0 and 0 / 26 / 0. Value against I2 at 120 and 30 minutes: +6 minutes-equivalent per visit (5%); break-even cost of a declared-wrong complaint 81 minutes at an undeclared cost of 30 (99 at 0). The tool's accuracy lead over I2 stayed positive in all 26 stress worlds (3.8 to 33 points, median 17). **Predictions: P2, P4, P7, P8, P9, P10, P12, P13 held; P1 (E at least 10% faster: it was level), P3 (E declares at least twice as often: 1.9x), P5, P6 (blind trees span only 1.7 points), P11, P14 (0.1058 against a bar of 0.1057) failed.** Caveats: n = 300, one informed author, home-field world, language-model authors, assumed cost model. The tool did not change.


## Addendum 44 (dated 2026-10-13, written BEFORE any arm was run; the proposals had been written and their MATCHES printed, but no accuracy number existed): does generating candidate causes the fixed model may have missed increase its accuracy? (simulation only; the only language-model use is three blind proposers)

**Question.** The shipped v1 tree has 14 causes (a fixed list). If a language model proposes causes the list lacks, each with a profile over the existing checks and one dedicated check, and those are added, does the tool get more accurate on complaints whose true cause is outside the list, and what does it cost on the complaints whose cause is on the list?

**Why this needs a hidden-cause world.** In every earlier world the truth is drawn from the tool's own list, so adding a cause can only take belief from the right answer (Addendum 37 did exactly that). The idea can only show a benefit in a world where some true causes are not on the list. Such a world is my construction: `HIDDEN` in `study_missed.py` has four causes (fire/smoke damper closed, supply-air temperature wrong, no return path, envelope/load beyond the box), each with a true response on existing checks and a true dedicated check, chosen by me from general practice and **not sourced**. A result is therefore a statement about this construction, not about buildings.

**Design.** Single complaints (the v1 engine, `simulate_v1`), min_rate 0.02 (the shipped default), tiers T2 and T3, 1500 complaints per cell, 4 values of m = P(true cause is hidden): 0, 0.05, 0.10 (primary), 0.20. Hidden complaints draw uniformly among the four; the rest draw from the tool's own prior. All arms see the same complaint and the same answer-stream seed. Arms: **A** the fixed model; **B_all** A + every proposed candidate with the proposer's own profile, share (capped 0.20) and check; **B_match** A + only the candidates the rubric matches to a hidden cause (isolates the decoy cost); **D** A + the hidden causes with TRUE profile and share but no dedicated check; **C** A + the hidden causes with true profile, share and dedicated check (oracle). Three blind proposers (fresh subagents given only the model's own cause and check lists, told to use their own knowledge) each write up to 6 candidates; each is its own B arm.

**Matching (a proposal can help only by matching).** A proposal matches a hidden cause by the regexes in `MATCH`. The first version (id + label + detail, first hit) mis-assigned one proposal (p3's "return/pressure imbalance" was matched to the fire damper because its detail says "fire/transfer damper"). **Deviation 1:** the primary rule is id + label first, detail only as fallback; the as-first-written rule is run as a sensitivity arm (`p3f`). Both assignments were printed before any accuracy run. A matched proposal takes the hidden cause's id; its dedicated check detects the matched hidden cause at that cause's TRUE hit rate (generous to B: it assumes the proposer's check is as good as mine); all other proposals are decoys the world never produces.

**Metrics (per complaint).** `ok_len` = top-ranked cause is the truth, or, for a hidden complaint, `other` (so the fixed model is not scored zero for saying "something else"); `ok` = exact. In-model accuracy = `ok_len` on non-hidden complaints. Wrong-and-confident = the tool declared a cause that is not the truth (nor `other` on a hidden complaint). Minutes as the tool charges them. Paired bootstrap over complaints (2000 resamples).

**Frozen decision rule (m = 0.10, each tier, each proposer separately).** The idea INCREASES ACCURACY for that proposer and tier only if all three hold for B_all against A: (1) overall `ok_len` gain with a 95% interval whose lower bound is above 0; (2) in-model accuracy loss at most 2 points (point estimate); (3) overall wrong-and-confident rise at most 1 point (point estimate). Verdict over the whole idea: YES if it holds for all three proposers at both tiers, NO if for none, otherwise MIXED. Safety overrides gain: failing (3) is a failure however large the gain, as in Addendum 37.

**Predictions (written before the run).** P1: B_match beats A overall at m = 0.10 (a real candidate with a real check helps). P2: B_all gains less than B_match (decoys cost). P3: D gains much less than C (naming a cause without a check to find it barely helps). P4: at m = 0 B_all is no better than A and is worse in-model (nothing to find, only decoys). (Not a prediction: the printed matches already showed that no proposer proposed `envelope_load`, so no B arm can name it; each proposer matches 3 of the 4 hidden causes.) P5: C's gain exceeds B_match's at m = 0.10 (it can also name the fourth cause). P6: the gain is smaller at T2 than T3 (more proposed checks need the air handler or the control system). I do not know which way (1)-(3) go for B_all; Addendum 37's decoy-like behaviour makes me expect (2) to be the fragile one.

**Not tested here.** Whether real missed causes look like these four; whether a language model's profile for a real cause is close to the truth (here the check hit rate is given the true value on a match); the joint (several complaints) tool; real technicians. Recall of the proposers (3 of 4 hidden causes each) is a property of my four choices, not a measured rate.

### Result of Addendum 44 (2026-10-13; 1500 complaints per cell, 8 cells, all gates passed: an empty proposal equals the fixed model exactly, the cause registry is restored, the matching rule behaves as written)

Output: `results/docmismatch/troubleshoot_missed/` (report.md, raw.pkl, the three proposals and their brief). **Verdict: NO.** At m = 0.10 no proposer passes at either tier: B_all against A loses **5.3 to 7.6 points overall** (T2 −5.3 to −6.8, T3 −5.7 to −7.6; all intervals below 0) and loses **5.1 to 9.6 points in-model**; wrong-and-confident does not rise (condition 3 holds) -- the failure is accuracy, not safety, the opposite of Addendum 37. The same under exact-match scoring (no credit for `other` on hidden complaints): B_all is −1.3 to −5.2 overall at m = 0.10, so the verdict does not depend on that scoring choice.

Where the idea does and does not work. **It does find the hidden causes**: on hidden complaints B_all names the true cause exactly 22-44% of the time (A cannot, by construction); at T3, B_match's lenient accuracy on hidden complaints is 72-74% against A's 37.5%, but at T2 it is no better than A (about 40% against 46%). But 90% of complaints are in-model, and every added cause costs those: even the oracle C, told the truth about all four hidden causes, loses 1.7 to 2.4 points in-model, and the matched proposals lose 2.7 to 4.9, because a cause added to the list takes belief from its neighbours and shares their checks. Net: B_match is level with A at m = 0.10 (−0.4, −0.3, +1.1; T3) and −5 at T2; at m = 0.20 it is +1.1 to +3.1 at T3 for the three proposers (two of three intervals above 0; T2 at m = 0.20 not examined here). At m = 0 (nothing to find) B_all loses 5.0 to 7.9 and even B_match 3.0 to 3.3 points at T3. Decoys are the bulk of the damage: B_all is 5 to 8 points worse than B_match at T3 m = 0.10 (each proposer wrote 2-3 candidates the world never produces; this is a cost only because my world lacks them: a world that had them would credit them). Even the oracle gains only +0.7 (T3) and +1.4 (T2) at m = 0.10, intervals including 0: with 10% hidden complaints and a full set of dedicated checks, a perfect cause list barely moves overall accuracy.

Predictions: P1 (B_match beats A at m = 0.10) **failed** (−0.4, −0.3, +1.1; T2 −5). P2 (decoys cost) held. P3 (D gains much less than C) not distinguishable at m = 0.10 (+0.4 against +0.7 overall; on hidden complaints D 56% against C 67%). P4 held (m = 0: all B arms worse). P5 (C above B_match at m = 0.10) held at T2, mixed at T3. P6 (T2 worse than T3) held, strongly (B_match −5 at T2 against about 0 at T3). Deviation 1 (matching) changed one proposer's assignment (p3 against p3f) and no verdict.

**What this does not show.** It does not show that missed causes are not worth adding in practice: it shows that at the registered prevalence (≤ 10% of complaints) and with a 12-check budget, a list grown by a blind language model's proposals loses accuracy on the complaints the tool already handles, in a world where the tool's own list generates the in-model truth (home field). The break-even prevalence is somewhere above 10% (positive at 20% for B_match at T3, and for B_all only under exact-match scoring and not for p3). Untested: a filter that keeps only candidates a technician or a data source has corroborated (B_match is such a filter given for free, and it is still not enough at 10%); adding a cause with a reduced share or only after a check fails to explain the complaint (an "open-world" fallback rather than a permanent list change); real missed causes (my four are not sourced and the proposers matched 3 of 4); a proposer's check hit rate being as good as the matched hidden cause's (granted here, generous to B).
